Cutoff Sobolev inequalities for local and non-local p p -energies on metric measure spaces
Meng Yang

Abstract
For p > 1 p>1 , we study subordination phenomena for local and non-local regular p p -energies on metric measure spaces. Under suitable geometric assumptions, we show that if a local regular p p -energy satisfies a Poincaré inequality together with a cutoff Sobolev inequality with scaling function Ψ \Psi , then all associated stable-like non-local p p -energies with scaling functions strictly below Ψ \Psi are regular and satisfy the corresponding non-local cutoff Sobolev inequalities. Moreover, if a stable-like non-local regular p p -energy with scaling function Ψ \Psi satisfies the corresponding non-local cutoff Sobolev inequality, then the same conclusion holds for all associated stable-like non-local p p -energies with scaling functions below Ψ \Psi . These results provide a non-linear extension of the classical subordination principle beyond the Dirichlet form framework.

Keywords
: subordination principle, Poincaré inequalities, cutoff Sobolev inequalities.

The author is very grateful to Jiaxin Hu for raising the question of comparing cutoff functions for local and non-local Dirichlet forms, which initiated this work, and to Eryan Hu for stimulating discussions on insightful techniques for non-local Dirichlet forms.

1
Introduction

Let us recall a classical result. Let
(X,d,m)
be a
d_{h}
-Ahlfors regular metric measure space. Assume that there exists a Brownian motion, or equivalently, a strongly local regular Dirichlet form, whose associated heat kernel satisfies the following two-sided sub-Gaussian estimates:

\frac{C_{1}}{t^{d_{h}/d_{w}}}\exp\left(-C_{2}\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{t^{1/d_{w}}}\right)^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\leq p_{t}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{3}}{t^{d_{h}/d_{w}}}\exp\left(-C_{4}\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{t^{1/d_{w}}}\right)^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right),

\text{HK}^{(L)}(d_{w})

where
d_{w}
is a new parameter called the walk dimension, which is typically strictly greater than 2 on fractals. By the classical subordination technique (see, for example,
[
34
,
40
]
), for any
\beta\in(0,d_{w})
, the stable-like quadratic form

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\beta)}(u)=\int_{X}\int_{X}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{2}}{d(x,y)^{d_{h}+\beta}}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

(1.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(\beta)}=\left\{u\in L^{2}(X;m):\int_{X}\int_{X}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{2}}{d(x,y)^{d_{h}+\beta}}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)<+\infty\right\},

defines a non-local regular Dirichlet form whose associated heat kernel satisfies the polynomial-type estimates

\frac{C_{1}}{t^{d_{h}/\beta}}\left(1+\frac{d(x,y)}{t^{1/\beta}}\right)^{-(d_{h}+\beta)}\leq p_{t}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{t^{d_{h}/\beta}}\left(1+\frac{d(x,y)}{t^{1/\beta}}\right)^{-(d_{h}+\beta)}.

\text{HK}^{(J)}(\beta)

Moreover, fix
\beta^{*}>0
and assume that there exists a non-local regular stable-like Dirichlet form
(\mathcal{E}^{(\beta^{*})},\mathcal{F}^{(\beta^{*})})
of the form (
1.1
), or equivalently, a stable-like jump process, whose heat kernel satisfies
\text{HK}^{(J)}(\beta^{*})
. Then, again by subordination, for any
\beta\in(0,\beta^{*}]
, the quadratic form
(\mathcal{E}^{(\beta)},\mathcal{F}^{(\beta)})
is a non-local regular Dirichlet form satisfying

⁢

HK

(
J
)

(
β
)

.

In 2004, Barlow and Bass
[
6
]
introduced the well-known cutoff Sobolev inequality
\text{CS}^{(L)}
in the local setting. This condition provides energy inequalities for cutoff functions associated with Dirichlet forms. They proved that

⁢

HK

(
L
)

(

d
w

)

is equivalent to the conjunction of the Poincaré inequality
\text{PI}^{(L)}(d_{w})
and
\text{CS}^{(L)}(d_{w})
, both with parameter
d_{w}
. In later work, Chen, Kumagai, Wang
[
17
,
18
]
, and independently Grigor’yan, Hu, Hu
[
25
]
, introduced the non-local condition
\text{CS}^{(J)}(\beta)
and proved that

⁢

HK

(
J
)

(
β
)

is equivalent to
\text{CS}^{(J)}(\beta)
.

The original formulation of
\text{CS}^{(L)}
is technically involved, even after several simplifications in
[
3
,
37
]
, and was long believed to be difficult to verify on concrete fractals, even in classical examples such as the Sierpiński gasket. In the strongly recurrent setting, roughly speaking when
d_{w}>d_{h}
, the author
[
46
]
provided a direct and simple proof of
\text{CS}^{(L)}(d_{w})
; see also
[
44
]
for the case of the Sierpiński gasket, where the main idea is illustrated.

It had remained a long-standing conjecture to replace
CS
by the substantially simpler capacity upper bound condition
\text{cap}_{\leq}
, known as the resistance conjecture (see
[
28
]
and
[
25
]
), until it was very recently resolved by Eriksson-Bique
[
21
]
in the local setting and by Murugan
[
38
]
in the non-local setting.

The preceding results are formulated within the Dirichlet form framework, where strongly local Dirichlet forms generalize the classical Dirichlet integral
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla f(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For general
p>1
, extending the classical
p
-energy
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla f(x)|^{p}\mathrm{d}x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, as initiated by
[
30
]
, the study of local
p
-energies on fractals and general metric measure spaces has been recently advanced considerably, see
[
13
,
42
,
9
,
39
,
32
,
14
,
2
,
5
]
. In this setting, a new parameter
d_{w,p}
, called the
p
-walk dimension, naturally arises; notably,
d_{w,2}
coincides with
d_{w}
in

⁢

HK

(
L
)

(

d
w

)

. One may therefore expect that suitable variants of the cutoff Sobolev inequality
\text{CS}^{(L)}
, as formulated in
[
48
]
, play a similarly fundamental role in the study of local
p
-energies. Some applications, such as the elliptic Harnack inequality, Wolff potential estimates, the singular behavior of energy measures, and the dichotomy of
p
-walk dimensions, were investigated in
[
46
,
45
,
47
]
.

By replacing
2
with
p
in (
1.1
), one can define stable-like non-local
p
-energy

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\beta,p)}(u)=\int_{X}\int_{X}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{d(x,y)^{d_{h}+\beta}}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

(1.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)}=\left\{u\in L^{p}(X;m):\int_{X}\int_{X}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{d(x,y)^{d_{h}+\beta}}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)<+\infty\right\}.

These forms are always well-defined for all
\beta>0
, and
\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)}
is decreasing in
\beta
. However, when
\beta
is too large, the space
\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)}
may be trivial, consisting only of constant functions. Similar to the Dirichlet form setting, we are therefore interested in the
regular
regime, where
\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)}
contains sufficiently many continuous functions to support potential-theoretic and PDE methods. Furthermore, one may formulate natural
p
-analogues of
\text{CS}^{(J)}
and
\text{cap}^{(J)}_{\leq}
, which likewise play a central role in the study of non-local
p
-energies.

Motivated by the subordination principle for heat kernels, we pose the following natural questions for
p
-energies.

Question 1
: Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L,p)},\mathcal{F}^{(L,p)})
be a local regular
p
-energy satisfying
\text{PI}^{(L)}(d_{w,p})
, and either
\text{CS}^{(L)}(d_{w,p})
or
\text{cap}^{(L)}(d_{w,p})_{\leq}
. Is it true that for any
\beta\in(0,d_{w,p})
, the form
(\mathcal{E}^{(\beta,p)},\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)})
defined by (
1.2
) is regular and satisfies
\text{CS}^{(J)}(\beta)
?

Question 2
: Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(\beta^{*},p)},\mathcal{F}^{(\beta^{*},p)})
be a non-local regular
p
-energy satisfying
\text{CS}^{(J)}(\beta^{*})
or
\text{cap}^{(J)}(\beta^{*})_{\leq}
. Is it true that for any
\beta\in(0,\beta^{*}]
, the form
(\mathcal{E}^{(\beta,p)},\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)})
is regular and satisfies
\text{CS}^{(J)}(\beta)
?

The main result of this paper provides affirmative answers to both questions, and in fact for more general scaling functions; see Theorem
2.8
. The key observation is that, under suitable geometric assumptions, if a regular
p
-energy, either local or non-local as in (
1.2
), satisfies
PI
and
CS
, then the cutoff functions appearing in
CS
can indeed be chosen to be
Hölder continuous
. These cutoff functions are closely related to the recently introduced condition
CE
in
[
4
]
, and also the original formulation of
CS
in
[
6
]
. In fact, we show that several versions of
CE
and
CS
are equivalent; see Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
.

For
p=2
, the above Hölder continuity follows from heat kernel estimates or equivalently from the parabolic Harnack inequality. For general
p>1
, due to non-linearity and the absence of a heat kernel, we reduce the argument to interior and boundary regularity theory for certain non-linear PDEs.

In Question 1, Hölder continuous cutoff functions for
\text{CS}^{(L)}(d_{w,p})
serve simultaneously as cutoff functions for
\text{CS}^{(J)}(\beta)
for
all

\beta\in(0,d_{w,p})
, and moreover ensure the regularity of
(\mathcal{E}^{(\beta,p)},\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)})
. A similar mechanism applies in Question 2. To the best of our knowledge, this relationship between cutoff functions associated with different
CS
conditions is new, even in the Dirichlet form setting. The connection between the continuity of cutoff functions and the regularity of the corresponding form was previously observed in
[
12
]
.

Finally, we discuss the
\text{cap}_{\leq}
condition. In Question 1, by an argument similar to that in
[
27
, Page 6432]
, cutoff functions for
\text{cap}^{(L)}(d_{w,p})_{\leq}
also serve as cutoff functions for
\text{cap}^{(J)}(\beta)_{\leq}
for all
\beta\in(0,d_{w,p})
. If
(\mathcal{E}^{(\beta,p)},\mathcal{F}^{(\beta,p)})
is assumed
a priori
to be regular, then the recent result of
[
38
]
implies that
\text{CS}^{(J)}(\beta)
holds. The regularity requirement naturally suggests a continuity condition for the cutoff functions. A similar regularity issue also arises in the context of Question 2.

Throughout this paper,
p\in(1,+\infty)
is fixed. The letters
C,C_{1},C_{2},C_{A},C_{B}
will always refer to some positive constants and may change at each occurrence. The sign
\asymp
means that the ratio of the two sides is bounded from above and below by positive constants. The sign
\lesssim
(
\gtrsim
) means that the LHS is bounded by positive constant times the RHS from above (below). For
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, denote
x\vee y=\max\{x,y\}
,
x\wedge y=\min\{x,y\}
,
x_{+}=\max\{x,0\}
, and
x_{-}=\max\{-x,0\}
. We use
\#A
to denote the cardinality of a set
A
.

2
Statement of main results

We say that a function
\Psi:[0,+\infty)\to[0,+\infty)
is doubling if
\Psi
is a homeomorphism, which implies that
\Psi
is strictly increasing continuous and
\Psi(0)=0
, and there exists
C_{\Psi}>1
, called a doubling constant of
\Psi
, such that
\Psi(2r)\leq C_{\Psi}\Psi(r)
for any
r>0
. Moreover, we assume that there exist
\beta^{(1)}_{\Psi},\beta^{(2)}_{\Psi}>0
with
\beta^{(1)}_{\Psi}\leq\beta^{(2)}_{\Psi}
such that

\frac{1}{C_{\Psi}}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{\beta_{\Psi}^{(1)}}\leq\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\leq{C_{\Psi}}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{\beta_{\Psi}^{(2)}}\text{ for any }r\leq R.

(2.1)

Indeed, we can take
\beta_{\Psi}^{(2)}=\log_{2}C_{\Psi}
. Throughout this paper, we always assume that
\Psi,\Upsilon
are doubling functions with doubling constants
C_{\Psi},C_{\Upsilon}
, and each of them satisfies Equation (
2.1
) with constants
\beta^{(1)}_{\Psi},\beta^{(2)}_{\Psi},\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon},\beta^{(2)}_{\Upsilon}
.

Let
(X,d,m)
be a complete unbounded metric measure space, that is,
(X,d)
is a complete unbounded locally compact separable metric space and
m
is a positive Radon measure on
X
with full support. Throughout this paper, we always assume that all metric balls are relatively compact. For any
x\in X
and any
r>0
, denote
B(x,r)=\{y\in X:d(x,y)<r\}
and
V(x,r)=m(B(x,r))
. If
B=B(x,r)
, then denote
\delta B=B(x,\delta r)
for any
\delta>0
. Let
\mathcal{B}(X)
be the family of all Borel measurable subsets of
X
. Let
C(X)
be the family of all continuous functions on
X
. Let
C_{c}(X)
be the family of all continuous functions on
X
with compact support. Denote
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{A}=\frac{1}{m(A)}\int_{A}
and
u_{A}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{A}u\mathrm{d}m
for any measurable set
A
with
m(A)\in(0,+\infty)
and any function
u
such that the integral
\int_{A}u\mathrm{d}m
is well-defined. We use the convention that
\int_{A}\int_{B}\ldots m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)
denotes integration over
x\in A
and
y\in B
. For a measurable function
u
on a measure set
A
, denote by
\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{A}u
and
\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{A}u
the essential supremum and essential infimum of
u
on
A
, respectively, and define
\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{A}=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{A}u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{A}u
whenever the difference is well-defined.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. We say that
V\subseteq X
is an
\varepsilon
-net if for any distinct
x,y\in V
, we have
d(x,y)\geq\varepsilon
, and for any
z\in X
, there exists
x\in V
such that
d(x,z)<\varepsilon
. Since
(X,d)
is separable, all
\varepsilon
-nets are countable.

Let
U,V
be two open subsets of
X
satisfying
U\subseteq\overline{U}\subseteq V
. We say that
\phi
is a cutoff function for
U\subseteq V
if
0\leq\phi\leq 1

m
-a.e. in
X
,
\phi=1

m
-a.e. in an open neighborhood of
\overline{U}
, and
\mathrm{supp}(\phi)\subseteq V
, where
\mathrm{supp}(f)
refers to the support of the measure of
|f|\mathrm{d}m
for any given function
f
. In our definition, we do
not
require cutoff functions to be continuous.

We say that the chain condition
CC
holds if there exists
C_{cc}>0
such that for any
x,y\in X
and any integer
n\geq 1
, there exists a sequence
\{x_{k}:0\leq k\leq n\}
of points in
X
with
x_{0}=x
and
x_{n}=y
such that

d(x_{k},x_{k-1})\leq C_{cc}\frac{d(x,y)}{n}\text{ for any }k=1,\ldots,n.

CC

Throughout this paper, we always assume
CC
. Since we will consider the boundary regularity of solutions to PDEs, the chain condition will play an important role in establishing certain regularity results; see Lemma
3.1
.

We say that the volume doubling condition
VD
holds if there exists
C_{VD}>0
such that

V(x,2r)\leq C_{VD}V(x,r)\text{ for any }x\in X,r>0.

VD

We say that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is a
p
-form if
\mathcal{F}
is a linear subspace of
L^{p}(X;m)
and
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{F}\to[0,+\infty)
satisfies that
\mathcal{E}^{1/p}
is a semi-norm on
\mathcal{F}
. In our definition, we allow a
p
-form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
to be trivial, in the sense that the domain
\mathcal{F}
consists only of constant functions.

For any
\lambda>0
, let
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{F}\to[0,+\infty)
be given by
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\cdot)=\mathcal{E}(\cdot)+\lVert\cdot\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}^{p}
.

We say that a
p
-form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is a
p
-energy if
\mathcal{F}
is dense in
L^{p}(X;m)
and satisfies the following conditions.

•

(Closed property)
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{E}_{1}^{1/p})
is a Banach space.

•

(Markovian property) For any
\varphi\in C(\mathbb{R})
with
\varphi(0)=0
and
|\varphi(t)-\varphi(s)|\leq|t-s|
for any
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
, we have
\varphi(f)\in\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{E}(\varphi(f))\leq\mathcal{E}(f)
.

•

(
p
-Clarkson’s inequality) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
, we have

\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}(f+g)+\mathcal{E}(f-g)\geq 2\left(\mathcal{E}(f)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\mathcal{E}(g)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in(1,2],\\
\mathcal{E}(f+g)+\mathcal{E}(f-g)\leq 2\left(\mathcal{E}(f)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\mathcal{E}(g)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in[2,+\infty).\\
\end{cases}

By the
p
-Clarkson’s inequality, for any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
, the derivative

\mathcal{E}(f;g)=\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}(f+tg)|_{t=0}\in\mathbb{R}

exists, the map
\mathcal{E}(f;\cdot):\mathcal{F}\to\mathbb{R}
is linear,
\mathcal{E}(f;f)=\mathcal{E}(f)
. Moreover, for any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
, for any
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathcal{E}(af;g)=\mathrm{sgn}(a)|a|^{p-1}\mathcal{E}(f;g),

|\mathcal{E}(f;g)|\leq\mathcal{E}(f)^{(p-1)/p}\mathcal{E}(g)^{1/p}.

Moreover, for any
\lambda>0
, all of the above results remain valid with
\mathcal{E}
replaced by
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
, and for any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
, we have

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(f;g)=\mathcal{E}(f;g)+\lambda\int_{X}\mathrm{sgn}(f)|f|^{p-1}g\mathrm{d}m.

See
[
31
, Theorem 3.7, Corollary 3.25]
for the proofs of these results.

We say that a
p
-form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is

•

strongly local if for any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
with compact support and
g
constant in an open neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}(f)
, we have
\mathcal{E}(f+g)=\mathcal{E}(f)+\mathcal{E}(g)
.

•

regular if
\mathcal{F}\cap C_{c}(X)
is uniformly dense in
C_{c}(X)
and
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{1/p}
-dense in
\mathcal{F}
.

For a doubling function
\Upsilon
satisfying Equation (
2.1
), we define a non-local
p
-form
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
associated with
\Upsilon
as follows. Let

\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)}(u)=\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J,\Upsilon)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

where
K^{(J,\Upsilon)}:X\times X\backslash\mathrm{diag}\to[0,+\infty)
satisfies that
K^{(J,\Upsilon)}(x,y)=K^{(J,\Upsilon)}(y,x)
for any distinct
x,y\in X
, and there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for any distinct
x,y\in X
, we have

\frac{C_{1}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}\leq K^{(J,\Upsilon)}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}.

\text{K}^{{(J)}}(\Upsilon)

Let

\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)}=\left\{u\in L^{p}(X;m):\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J,\Upsilon)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)<+\infty\right\},

and

\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J,\Upsilon)}=\left\{u+a:u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)},a\in\mathbb{R}\right\}.

For any
x_{0}\in X
and any
R>0
, for any measurable function
u
, define

\mathrm{Tail}^{(J,\Upsilon)}(u;x_{0},R)=\left(\Upsilon(R)\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R)}\lvert u(y)\rvert^{p-1}K^{(J,\Upsilon)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{1/(p-1)}.

For any measurable subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, for any
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J,\Upsilon)}
, let
\Gamma^{(J,\Upsilon)}_{\Omega}(u)
be the measure on
X
given by

\int_{X}f\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J,\Upsilon)}_{\Omega}(u)=\int_{X}\left(\int_{\Omega}f(x)\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J,\Upsilon)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)\text{ for any }f\in C_{c}(X).

For simplicity, we also write
\mathcal{E}^{(J)},K^{(J)},\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}
, etc. to denote
\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},K^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathrm{Tail}^{(J,\Upsilon)}
, etc. when there is no ambiguity.

We say that the non-local cutoff Sobolev inequality
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
holds if there exist
\delta\in(0,1)
,
C,C_{1},C_{2}>0
,

\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert\leq C\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta},

\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}(\phi)\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}(f)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

We say that
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)

We note that the condition

⁢

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

[

, Definition 2.1]
and the CSJ condition introduced in
[

, DEFINITION 1.5]
, which are formulated in the setting of Dirichlet forms (
p=2
), in the following aspects:

(a)

We only consider cutoff functions for
B(x,r)\subseteq B(x,A_{1}r)
for any
r>0
, instead of cutoff functions for
B(x,R)\subseteq B(x,R+r)
for any
R,r>0
, as required in the AB condition, or cutoff functions for
B(x,R)\subseteq B(x,R+r)
for any
R\geq r>0
, as required in the CSJ condition.

(b)

For the term on the LHS and the second term on the RHS, we only consider integrals over
B(x,A_{2}r)
for any
r>0
, instead of integrals over
B(x,R^{\prime})
for any
R^{\prime}>R+r
, as required in the AB condition, or over
B(x,R+(1+C_{0})r)
for some
C_{0}\in(0,1]
, as required in the CSJ condition.

(c)

For the first term on the RHS, we only consider integrals over the “largest” balls
B(x,A_{2}r)
, instead of integrals over balls
B(x,R+r)
, as required in the AB condition, or over annuli, as required in the CSJ condition.

By a standard covering argument and a self-improvement argument, we will show that

(

for local

Let

Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
be a strongly local regular
p
-energy. By
[
41
, Theorem 1.4]
, there exists a (canonical)
p
-energy measure
\Gamma^{(L)}:\mathcal{F}^{(L)}\times\mathcal{B}(X)\to[0,+\infty)
,
(f,A)\mapsto\Gamma^{(L)}(f)(A)
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

For any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
,
\Gamma^{(L)}(f)(\cdot)
is a positive Radon measure on
X
with
\Gamma^{(L)}(f)(X)=\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(f)
.

(2)

For any
A\in\mathcal{B}(X)
,
\Gamma^{(L)}(\cdot)(A)^{1/p}
is a semi-norm on
\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
.

(3)

For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}\cap C_{c}(X)
,
A\in\mathcal{B}(X)
, if
f-g
is constant on
A
, then
\Gamma^{(L)}(f)(A)=\Gamma^{(L)}(g)(A)
.

(4)

(
p
-Clarkson’s inequality) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
, for any
A\in\mathcal{B}(X)
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(L)}(f+g)(A)+\Gamma^{(L)}(f-g)(A)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\geq 2\left(\Gamma^{(L)}(f)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\Gamma^{(L)}(g)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in(1,2],\\
\leq 2\left(\Gamma^{(L)}(f)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\Gamma^{(L)}(g)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in[2,+\infty).\\
\end{cases}

(5)

(Chain rule) For any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}\cap C_{c}(X)
, for any piecewise
C^{1}
function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\varphi(f))=|\varphi^{\prime}(f)|^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(f)
.

We say that the local Poincaré inequality

⁢

(

)

(

\text{PI}^{({L})}(\Psi)

We say that the local cutoff Sobolev inequality

\text{CS}^{(L)}(\Psi)\text{-}1

\int_{B(x_{0},A_{S}r)}|\widetilde{f}|^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x_{0},A_{S}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(f)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},A_{S}r)}|f|^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

(2.4)

For
\bullet\in\{L,J\}
, let

we have

\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert\leq C\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta},

\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)\text{-}1

\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)\leq C\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s\wedge r)},

\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)\text{-}2

where
\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}=\Gamma^{(L)}
for
\bullet=L
and
\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}=\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}
for
\bullet=J
. We say that
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{cont}}(\Psi)
holds if

⁢

(
∙
)

(

)

-

holds and the cutoff function

⁢

(

)

(

)

-

holds.

For
\square\in\{\text{strong},\text{cont},\text{weak}\}
, if
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}(\Psi)
holds with some
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
, then it also holds for any
\delta_{2}\in(0,\delta_{1}]
. Hence, if necessary, we may take
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small. The precise value of
\delta
is unimportant; what matters is the existence of such a
\delta
.

The first main results of this paper establish equivalences between various CE and CS conditions in both the local and non-local settings.

Theorem 2.1
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
be a strongly local regular
p
-energy satisfying

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

. Then

\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{cont}}(\Psi)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)

\Leftrightarrow
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{cont}}(\Psi)

\Leftrightarrow
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
.

Theorem 2.2
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
be a non-local regular
p
-energy associated with
\Upsilon
. Then

\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)

\Leftrightarrow
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
\Leftrightarrow

\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
.

Remark 2.3
.

By the very recent results of
[
21
]
and
[
38
]
, the condition

⁢

cap

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

≤

can now be added to the list of equivalent conditions in Theorem
2.1
. Likewise, the condition

⁢

cap

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

≤

can now be added to the equivalences in Theorem
2.2
.

Remark 2.4
.

We will prove the diagrams of implications as in Figure
1
.

\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{cont}}
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{weak}}
\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}
\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{cont}}
\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{weak}}
\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{weak}}
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}

Figure 1
:
Strategy of the proof of “
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}\Leftrightarrow\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}
”

The implications “
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}\Rightarrow\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{cont}}\Rightarrow\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{weak}}
” and “
\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}\Rightarrow\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{cont}}\Rightarrow\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{weak}}
” follow trivially from the definitions. For
\square\in\{\text{strong},\text{cont},\text{weak}\}
, we will prove “
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}\Rightarrow\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}
” with the cutoff functions provided by
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}
and following the idea of
[
4
, Proposition 4.10]
. Moreover, the implication “
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\square}\Rightarrow\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\square}
” will be proved under the sole assumption that
(\mathcal{E}^{{(J)}},\mathcal{F}^{(J)})
is a non-local
p
-form. The key novelty of our result lies in the proof of “
\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{weak}}\Rightarrow\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}
”. We will reduce the proof to the interior and boundary regularity of solutions to certain PDEs.

Remark 2.5
.

For
p=2
, the conjunction of

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

and
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
is equivalent to the following two-sided heat kernel estimates

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}}{V\left(x,\Psi^{-1}(t)\right)}\exp\left(-\Lambda\left(C_{2}d(x,y),t\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq p_{t}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{3}}{V\left(x,\Psi^{-1}(t)\right)}\exp\left(-\Lambda\left(C_{4}d(x,y),t\right)\right),

HK
(
\Psi
)

where

\Lambda(R,t)=\sup_{s\in(0,+\infty)}\left(\frac{R}{s}-\frac{t}{\Psi(s)}\right),

see
[
29
, Theorem 1.2]
, which yield Hölder continuity of
p_{t}(x,y)
; see, for example,
[
7
, COROLLARY 4.2]
, where this is obtained via the parabolic Harnack inequality, which in turn intrinsically implies the existence of some
\delta>0
in
\text{CE}_{\text{strong}}^{{(L)}}(\Psi)
. However, for general
p>1
, due to non-linearity and the absence of a heat kernel, we will need to establish Hölder regularity using a different approach.

The second main result of this paper shows that a non-local
p
-form satisfying
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}
or
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}
is indeed a
regular

p
-energy, as stated below; see
[
12
]
for the corresponding result in the linear case
p=2
.

Proposition 2.6
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
be a non-local
p
-form associated with
\Upsilon
. If either
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
or
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
holds, then
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
is a regular
p
-energy.

By Remark
2.4
, we have the following observations. First, it suffices to prove Proposition
2.6
under the assumption
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
. Second, we have the following direct consequence for non-local
p
-forms from Theorem
2.2
.

Corollary 2.7
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
be a non-local
p
-form associated with
\Upsilon
. Then

\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)

\Leftrightarrow

\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
.

Let
\Psi,\Upsilon
be doubling functions satisfying Equation (
2.1
). We say that
\Upsilon
grows strictly slower than
\Psi
, or that
(\Upsilon,\Psi)
satisfies the strict upper growth condition, denoted by

⁢
SUG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

, if there exists an increasing function
\Theta:[0,1]\to[0,+\infty)
with
\Theta(1)\geq 1
and
\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\Theta(\frac{1}{2^{n}})<+\infty
such that

\frac{\Upsilon(R)}{\Upsilon(r)}\leq\Theta\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\text{ for any }r\leq R.

\text{SUG}(\Upsilon,\Psi)

We say that
\Upsilon
grows no faster than
\Psi
, or that
(\Upsilon,\Psi)
satisfies the upper growth condition, denoted by

⁢
UG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

, if there exists
C\geq 1
such that

\frac{\Upsilon(R)}{\Upsilon(r)}\leq C\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\text{ for any }r\leq R.

\text{UG}(\Upsilon,\Psi)

As the names suggest,

⁢
SUG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

implies

⁢
UG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

.

The third and central main result of this paper consists of the following two “subordination” results: one from a local form to a non-local form, and the other from a non-local form to another non-local form.

Theorem 2.8
.

Assume
VD
.

(a)

Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
be a strongly local regular
p
-energy satisfying

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,
\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
. Let
\Upsilon
be a doubling function satisfying

⁢
SUG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

and
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
a non-local
p
-form associated with
\Upsilon
. Then
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
is a regular
p
-energy satisfying
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
.

(b)

Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Psi)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Psi)})
be a non-local regular
p
-energy associated with
\Psi
that satisfies
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
. Let
\Upsilon
be a doubling function satisfying

⁢
UG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

and
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
a non-local
p
-form associated with
\Upsilon
. Then
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
is a regular
p
-energy satisfying
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
.

Remark 2.9
.

Roughly speaking, we prove that
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
can be “subordinated” to
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
, with both

⁢

CE

(
∙
)

(
Ψ
)

-
1

and

⁢

CE

(
∙
)

(
Ψ
)

-
2

playing a crucial role in the argument.

In
(a)
, by Theorem
2.1
, the assumption
\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
can be replaced by
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
, or by any other equivalent condition stated therein.

In
(b)
, by Theorem
2.2
, the assumption
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
can be replaced by
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
, or by any other equivalent condition stated therein.

Moreover, in
(b)
, by Corollary
2.7
, the assumption that
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Psi)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Psi)})
is a regular
p
-energy satisfying
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
can be further replaced by the assumption that it is a
p
-form satisfying
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Psi)
, or by any other equivalent condition stated therein.

This paper is organized as follows. In Section
3
, we collect some preliminary results for later use. In Section
4
, we prove the self-improvement property of
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}
and
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}
. In Section
5
, we give the proof of Proposition
2.6
. In Section
6
, we give the proof of Theorem
2.8
. In Section
7
, we give the proof of “
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}\Rightarrow\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}
” in Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
. In Section
8
, we give the proof of “
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}
\Rightarrow
\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}
” in Theorem
2.1
. In Section
9
, we give two oscillation inequalities. In Section
10
, we give the proof of “
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}
\Rightarrow
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}
” in Theorem
2.2
. In Section
11
, we give some elementary results used in the previous sections.

3
Preliminary

Let
\Omega\subseteq X
be an open subset and
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
. Let
c,r_{0}>0
. We say that
X\backslash\Omega
has a
(c,r_{0})
-corkscrew at
x_{0}
if for any
r\in(0,r_{0})
,
B(x_{0},r)\backslash U
contains a ball with radius
cr
. Obviously, if a
(c,r_{0})
-corkscrew exists, then for any
A\geq 1
, a
(\frac{1}{A}c,Ar_{0})
-corkscrew and a
(\frac{1}{A}c,r_{0})
-corkscrew also exist.

Lemma 3.1

(
[
11
, Lemma 14.2]
; see also
[
45
, Proposition 3.12]
)
.

Assume
CC
. Then for any
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
with
\delta_{1}<\delta_{2}
, there exists
c>0
depending only on
C_{cc},\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
such that for any ball
B=B(x_{0},r)
, there exists an open set
\Omega
with
\delta_{1}B\subseteq\Omega\subseteq\delta_{2}B
such that, for any
x\in\partial\Omega
, we have
X\backslash\Omega
has a
(c,r)
-corkscrew at
x
, and hence a
(\frac{1}{A}c,Ar)
-corkscrew at
x
for any
A\geq 1
.

We need the following results concerning BMO spaces. Let
U
be an open set and
u
a locally integrable function in
U
. We define the semi-norm
\lVert{u}\rVert_{\mathrm{BMO}(U)}
as

\lVert{u}\rVert_{\mathrm{BMO}(U)}=\sup\left\{\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B}|u-u_{B}|\mathrm{d}m:B\subseteq U\text{ is a ball}\right\}.

Let
\mathrm{BMO}(U)
be the family of all locally integrable functions
u
in
U
with
\lVert{u}\rVert_{\mathrm{BMO}(U)}<+\infty
.

Lemma 3.2

(John-Nirenberg inequality;
[
1
, THEOREM 5.2]
)
.

Assume
VD
. Then there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for any open set
U
and any
u\in\mathrm{BMO}(U)
, for any ball
B
with
12B\subseteq U
and any
\lambda>0
, we have

m\left(B\cap\{|u-u_{B}|>\lambda\}\right)\leq C_{1}m(B)\exp\left(-C_{2}\frac{\lambda}{\lVert{u}\rVert_{\mathrm{BMO}(U)}}\right).

Lemma 3.3

(
[
10
, COROLLARY 5.6]
)
.

Assume
VD
. Then for any ball
B(x_{0},R)
and any
u\in\mathrm{BMO}(B(x_{0},R))
, for any ball
B
with
12B\subseteq B(x_{0},R)
, for any
b\geq\lVert{u}\rVert_{\mathrm{BMO}(B(x_{0},R))}
, we have

\left\{\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B}\exp\left(\frac{C_{2}}{2b}u\right)\mathrm{d}m\right\}\left\{\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{2}}{2b}u\right)\mathrm{d}m\right\}\leq(C_{1}+1)^{2},

where
C_{1},C_{2}
are the positive constants appearing in Lemma
3.2
.

For
\bullet\in\{L,J\}
, let
(\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)},\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)})
be a
regular

p
-energy. Following
[
48
, Section 8]
and
[
46
, Section 6]
, we can define the capacity
\mathrm{cap}^{(\bullet)}_{1}
, and the notion of
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}_{1}
-quasi-continuity, and obtain the corresponding results. Although the arguments in
[
48
]
were presented in the local setting, the same line of reasoning also applies to the non-local setting. For simplicity, when no ambiguity arises, we write quasi-continuous and q.e. to mean
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}_{1}
-quasi-continuous and
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}_{1}
-quasi-everywhere (abbreviated
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}_{1}
-q.e.), respectively.

Lemma 3.4

(
[
48
, Proposition 8.5]
)
.

Each
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}
admits a quasi-continuous modification
\widetilde{u}
, that is,
\widetilde{u}
is quasi-continuous and
u=\widetilde{u}

m
-a.e. in
X
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq X
be an open subset. Define

\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}(\Omega)=\text{the }\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}_{1}\text{-closure of }\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}\cap C_{c}(\Omega).

We say that
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}
is harmonic in
\Omega
if
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}(u;\varphi)=0
for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}\cap C_{c}(\Omega)
, denoted by
-\Delta_{p}^{(\bullet)}u=0
in
\Omega
. We say that
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}
is superharmonic in
\Omega
(resp. subharmonic in
\Omega
) if
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}(u;\varphi)\geq 0
(resp.
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}(u;\varphi)\leq 0
) for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}\cap C_{c}(\Omega)
, denoted by
-\Delta_{p}^{(\bullet)}u\geq 0
in
\Omega
(resp.
-\Delta_{p}^{(\bullet)}u\leq 0
in
\Omega
). More generally, let
f\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)
, we write
-\Delta_{p}^{(\bullet)}u=f
(resp.
-\Delta_{p}^{(\bullet)}u\geq f
) if
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}(u;\varphi)=\int_{X}f\varphi\mathrm{d}m
(resp.
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}(u;v)\geq\int_{X}f\varphi\mathrm{d}m
) for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}\cap C_{c}(\Omega)
. If
\Omega
is additionally assumed to be bounded, then by the following result, the above (in)equalities also hold for any non-negative
v\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}(\Omega)
.

Lemma 3.5

(
[
46
, Lemma 6.2]
)
.

For any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}(\Omega)=\left\{u\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}:\widetilde{u}=0\text{ q.e. on }X\backslash\Omega\right\}.

We establish existence and uniqueness results for boundary value problems associated with equations involving
\Delta_{p}^{(\bullet)}
, as follows. The proof follows the same direct method in the calculus of variations as in
[
36
, Theorem 8]
, which is stated in the setting of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Owing to the
p
-Clarkson’s inequality for
p>1
, the same argument applies in our setting, and the proof is therefore omitted.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq X
be a bounded open subset and let
\lambda\geq 0
. Assume
VD
,
\text{PI}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)
for some
\Psi
in the case
\lambda=0
. Then there exists a unique
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}(\Omega)
such that
-\Delta_{p}^{(\bullet)}u+\lambda\lvert u\rvert^{p-2}u=1
in
\Omega
, that is,

\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}(u;\varphi)+\lambda\int_{X}\lvert u\rvert^{p-2}u\varphi\mathrm{d}m=\int_{X}\varphi\mathrm{d}m,

for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}(\Omega)
.

Remark 3.7
.

In the case
\lambda=0
,
VD
,
\text{PI}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)
ensure that
(\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}(\Omega),(\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)})^{1/p})
is a Banach space. In contrast, when
\lambda>0
,
(\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}(\Omega),(\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}_{\lambda})^{1/p})
is automatically a Banach space by definition.

We have the following local and non-local comparison principles.

Proposition 3.8

(Comparison principle, local version;
[
45
, Proposition 3.6]
)
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
be a strongly local regular
p
-energy satisfying

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

. Let
\lambda\geq 0
. Let
\Omega\subseteq X
be a bounded open subset and let
u,v\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
satisfy

-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}u+\lambda|u|^{p-2}u\geq-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}v+\lambda|v|^{p-2}v\text{ in }\Omega,

that is,

\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(u;\varphi)+\lambda\int_{X}|u|^{p-2}u\varphi\mathrm{d}m\geq\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(v;\varphi)+\lambda\int_{X}|v|^{p-2}v\varphi\mathrm{d}m

for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(\Omega)
, and
(v-u)_{+}\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(\Omega)
, or

\widetilde{u}\geq\widetilde{v}\text{ q.e. on }W\backslash\Omega\text{ for some open set }W\supseteq\overline{\Omega}\supseteq\Omega.

Then
u\geq v
in
\Omega
.

Proposition 3.9

(Comparison principle, non-local version)
.

Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(J)},\mathcal{F}^{(J)})
be a non-local regular
p
-energy associated with
\Upsilon
. Let
\lambda\geq 0
. Let
\Omega\subseteq X
be a bounded open subset and let
u,v\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
satisfy

-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u+\lambda\lvert u\rvert^{p-2}u\geq-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}v+\lambda\lvert v\rvert^{p-2}v\text{ in }\Omega,

that is,

\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u;\varphi)+\lambda\int_{X}\lvert u\rvert^{p-2}u\varphi\mathrm{d}m\geq\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(v;\varphi)+\lambda\int_{X}\lvert v\rvert^{p-2}v\varphi\mathrm{d}m

for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)
, and

\widetilde{u}\geq\widetilde{v}\text{ q.e. on }X\backslash\Omega.

Then
u\geq v
in
\Omega
, that is,
u\geq v
in
X
.

Remark 3.10
.

Proposition
3.9
was proved in a different setting in
[
35
, Lemma 9]
. The same argument applies in our setting, and therefore the proof is omitted.

We have the following local and non-local Sobolev inequalities.

Proposition 3.11

(Sobolev inequality, local version;
[
45
, Proposition 4.1]
)
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
be a strongly local regular
p
-energy satisfying

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

. Then there exist
\kappa>1
,
C>0
such that for any ball
B(x_{0},R)
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(B(x_{0},R))
, we have

\left(\int_{B(x_{0},R)}|f|^{p\kappa}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\leq C\frac{\Psi(R)}{V(x_{0},R)^{\frac{\kappa-1}{\kappa}}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(f).

Indeed, we can take
\kappa=\frac{\nu}{\nu-\beta_{\Psi}^{(1)}}
, where
\nu=\max\{\beta_{\Psi}^{(1)}+1,\log_{2}C_{VD}\}
.

Proposition 3.12

(Sobolev inequality, non-local version;
[
15
, Proposition 3.5, Definition 3.6, Theorem 3.8]
)
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(J)},\mathcal{F}^{(J)})
be a non-local regular
p
-energy associated with
\Upsilon
. Then there exist
\kappa>1
,
C>0
such that for any ball
B(x_{0},R)
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(B(x_{0},R))
, we have

\left(\int_{B(x_{0},R)}|f|^{p\kappa}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\leq C\frac{\Upsilon(R)}{V(x_{0},R)^{\frac{\kappa-1}{\kappa}}}\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(f).

Indeed, we can take
\kappa=\frac{\nu}{\nu-\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}
, where
\nu=\max\{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}+1,\log_{2}C_{VD}\}
.

4
Self-improvement of
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}
and
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}

In this section, we prove the self-improvement property of
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
and
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
; see also
[
37
, Lemma 6.2]
and
[
46
, Proposition 3.1]
for analogous results in the local setting, and
[
25
, Lemma 2.9]
and
[
18
, PROPOSITION 2.4]
in the non-local setting.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\varepsilon}>0
depending on
\varepsilon
, such that for any
x_{0}\in X
, for any
R,r,R^{\prime}>0
with
R+r<R^{\prime}
, there exists a cutoff function
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},R+r)
such that for any
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\phi)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\lvert\phi\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(4.1)

Moreover, if
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
holds, then we can take
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
.

Proof.

Firstly, we prove that there exists
C>0
such that for any
x_{0}\in X
, for any
R,r,R^{\prime}>0
with
R+r<R^{\prime}
, there exists a cutoff function
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},R+r)
such that for any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\phi)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{C}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(4.2)

Let
A_{1},A_{2},C_{1},C_{2}
be the constants in

⁢

CS

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

-
2

,
L=2(A_{1}+8A_{2}+5)+1
, and
V
an
(\frac{1}{L}r)
-net. For any
v\in V
, by
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
, there exists a cutoff function
\phi_{v}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
for
B(v,\frac{1}{L}r)\subseteq B(v,\frac{A_{1}}{L}r)
such that

\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}(\phi_{v})\leq C_{1}\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}(f)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

We claim that there exists
C_{3}>0
such that for any open set
\Omega\supseteq B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)
, we have

\int_{\Omega}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{\Omega}(\phi_{v})\leq C_{3}\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}(f)+\frac{C_{3}}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(4.3)

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{\Omega}(\phi_{v})=\left(\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}+\int_{\Omega\backslash B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\right)\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{\Omega}(\phi_{v})=I_{1}+I_{2},

and
I_{1}=I_{11}+I_{12}
, where

\displaystyle I_{11}=\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}(\phi_{v})\lesssim\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

and by

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle I_{12}=\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\int_{\Omega\backslash B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{1}}{L}r)}\int_{\Omega\backslash B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\phi_{v}(x)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{1}}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,\frac{A_{2}-A_{1}}{L}r)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{1}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

and

\displaystyle I_{2}=\int_{\Omega\backslash B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega\backslash B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\int_{B(v,\frac{A_{1}}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\phi_{v}(y)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega\backslash B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,\frac{A_{2}-A_{1}}{L}r)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{\Omega\backslash B(v,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

hence we have Equation (
4.3
).

Let
V_{1}=V\cap B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)
, then by
VD
, we have
\#V_{1}<+\infty
. Let

\phi=\max_{v\in V_{1}}\phi_{v},

then
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
satisfies that
0\leq\phi\leq 1
in
X
,
\phi=1
in
B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r-\frac{1}{L}r)\supseteq\overline{B(x_{0},R)}
, and
\mathrm{supp}(\phi)\subseteq B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{A_{1}}{L}r)\subseteq B(x_{0},R+r)
, hence
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
is a cutoff function for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},R+r)
. Moreover

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\phi)=\iint\limits_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})\times B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\left(\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}B(x_{0},R^{\prime})\times B(x_{0},R^{\prime})\\
d(x,y)\geq\frac{1}{L}r\end{subarray}}+\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}B(x_{0},R^{\prime})\times B(x_{0},R^{\prime})\\
d(x,y)<\frac{1}{L}r\end{subarray}}\right)\ldots\mathrm{d}m\mathrm{d}m=I_{3}+I_{4}.

By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle I_{3}\leq\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}B(x_{0},R^{\prime})\times B(x_{0},R^{\prime})\\
d(x,y)\geq\frac{1}{L}r\end{subarray}}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,\frac{1}{L}r)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

For any
x,y\in X
with
d(x,y)<\frac{1}{L}r
, we have
\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert\neq 0
only if
x,y\in U
, where

U=B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{A_{1}+1}{L}r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r-\frac{2}{L}r)}.

Let

\displaystyle V_{2}=V\cap\left(B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r)\backslash B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r-\frac{3}{L}r)\right),

then
U\subseteq\cup_{v\in V_{2}}B(v,\frac{1}{L}r)
, hence

\displaystyle I_{4}=\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\times U\\
d(x,y)<\frac{1}{L}r\end{subarray}}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in V_{2}}\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}B(v,\frac{1}{L}r)\times U\\
d(x,y)<\frac{1}{L}r\end{subarray}}\ldots\mathrm{d}m\mathrm{d}m\leq\sum_{v\in V_{2}}\iint_{B(v,\frac{1}{L}r)\times B(v,\frac{2}{L}r)}\ldots\mathrm{d}m\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in V_{2}}\iint_{B(v,\frac{2}{L}r)\times B(v,\frac{2}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y).

By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
M
depending only on
C_{VD},A_{1},L
such that

\#\left\{w\in V:d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\right\}\leq M\text{ for any }v\in V.

For any
v\in V_{2}
, in
B(v,\frac{2}{L}r)
, we have

\phi=\max_{w\in V_{1}}\phi_{w}=\max\left\{\phi_{w}:w\in V_{1},d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\right\}.

(4.4)

By the elementary fact that
\lvert a_{1}\vee a_{2}-a_{3}\vee a_{4}\rvert\leq\lvert a_{1}-a_{3}\rvert+\lvert a_{2}-a_{4}\rvert
for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\mathbb{R}
, for any
x,y\in B(v,\frac{2}{L}r)
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}\leq\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in V_{1}\\
d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\end{subarray}}\lvert\phi_{w}(x)-\phi_{w}(y)\rvert\right)^{p}\leq M^{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in V_{1}\\
d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\end{subarray}}\lvert\phi_{w}(x)-\phi_{w}(y)\rvert^{p}.

Hence

\displaystyle I_{4}\leq M^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in V_{1}\\
d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\end{subarray}}\iint_{B(v,\frac{2}{L}r)\times B(v,\frac{2}{L}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{w}(x)-\phi_{w}(y)\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0pt\cdot K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq M^{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in V_{2},w\in V_{1}\\
d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\end{subarray}}\iint_{B(w,\frac{A_{1}+4}{L}r)\times B(w,\frac{A_{1}+4}{L}r)}\ldots\mathrm{d}m\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle\leq M^{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in V_{2},w\in V_{1}\\
d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\end{subarray}}\iint_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)\times B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\ldots\mathrm{d}m\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=M^{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in V_{2},w\in V_{1}\\
d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\end{subarray}}\int_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}(\phi_{w}).

Let

\displaystyle V_{3}=V_{1}\backslash B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r-\frac{A_{1}+5}{L}r),

then

\displaystyle I_{4}\leq M^{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in V_{2},w\in V_{3}\\
d(v,w)<\frac{A_{1}+2}{L}r\end{subarray}}\int_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}(\phi_{w})

\displaystyle\leq M^{p}\sum_{w\in V_{3}}\int_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}(\phi_{w}).

Taking
v=w
and
\Omega=B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)
in Equation (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}(\phi_{w})

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{B(w,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(w,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

hence

\displaystyle I_{4}\lesssim\sum_{w\in V_{3}}\left(\int_{B(w,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{X}\left(\sum_{w\in V_{3}}1_{B(w,\frac{A_{2}}{L}r)}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{X}\left(\sum_{w\in V_{3}}1_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\right)\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
N
depending only on
C_{VD},A_{2},L
such that

\sum_{w\in V_{3}}1_{B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\leq N1_{\cup_{w\in V_{3}}B(w,\frac{8A_{2}}{L}r)}\leq N1_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}},

hence

I_{4}\lesssim\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\phi)=I_{3}+I_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

Secondly, we prove Equation (
4.1
). Let
a\in(0,1)
be chosen later. For any
n\geq 1
, let
s_{n}=\frac{1-a}{a}a^{n}r
, then
\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}s_{n}=r
. Let
r_{0}=0
and
r_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}s_{k}
for any
n\geq 1
, then
r_{n}\uparrow r
. For any
n\geq 0
, let
B_{n}=B(x_{0},R+r_{n})
, then
B(x_{0},R)=B_{0}\subseteq B_{n}\uparrow B(x_{0},R+r)
. For any
n\geq 0
, by Equation (
4.2
), there exists a cutoff function
\phi_{n}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
for
B_{n}\subseteq B_{n+1}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\phi_{n})

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{B_{n+1}\backslash\overline{B_{n}}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B_{n+1}\backslash\overline{B_{n}}}(f)+\frac{C}{\Upsilon(s_{n+1})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(4.5)

Let
b\in(0,1)
be chosen later,
b_{n}=b^{n}-b^{n+1}
for any
n\geq 0
, and

\phi=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}b_{k}\phi_{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}(b^{k}-b^{k+1})\phi_{k},

(4.6)

then
0\leq\phi\leq 1
in

,
\phi=1
in

, and
\phi=0
on

. For any

, in
B_{n+1}
, we have
\phi=\sum_{k=0}^{n}(b^{k}-b^{k+1})\phi_{k}+b^{n+1}\geq b^{n+1}
; in

, we have
\phi=(b^{n}-b^{n+1})\phi_{n}+b^{n+1}
, hence
b^{n+1}\leq\phi\leq b^{n}
in
B_{n+1}\backslash\overline{B_{n}}
.

For any
n\geq 0
, let
\varphi_{n}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}\phi_{k}
. By Fatou’s lemma, we only need to show that for suitable choices of
a
and
b
depending on
\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\varphi_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\lvert\phi\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

for any
n>p
, where
C_{\varepsilon}>0
is independent of
n
.

Let
p=p_{0}+\delta_{0}
, where
1\leq p_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\delta_{0}\in[0,1)
. We have the following three cases:

(a)

p_{0}\geq 2
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
,

(b)

p_{0}\geq 2
and
\delta_{0}=0
,

(c)

p_{0}=1
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
.

In case
(a)
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\varphi_{n}(x)-\varphi_{n}(y)\rvert^{p}\leq\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{p_{0}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}},

where

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}},

and

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{p_{0}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\in\{0,\ldots,p_{0}\}\\
t_{0}+\ldots+t_{n}=p_{0}\end{subarray}}\prod_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{t_{k}}}.

For any sequence
\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}\in\{0,\ldots,p_{0}\}^{n+1}
with
t_{0}+\ldots+t_{n}=p_{0}
, let

I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})=\left\{k:t_{k}\geq 1\right\}\subseteq\left\{0,\ldots,n\right\},

and

i(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})=\max\left\{k\in I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})\supseteq\{i\in\mathbb{Z}:l\leq i\leq k\}\\
\text{for any $l\in I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})$ with $l\leq k$}\end{array}\right\},

then
1\leq\#I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})\leq p_{0}
. We say that
\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}
is connected if
I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})
consists of consecutive integers, or equivalently,
i(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})=\max\{k:k\in I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})\}
; otherwise we say that it is disconnected, see Table
1
for two examples with
n=4
and
p_{0}=3
. Obviously, if
\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}
is disconnected, then
k=i(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})\leq n-2
and
I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})\cap\{k+2,k+3,\ldots\}\neq\emptyset
.

Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\varphi_{n})

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{connected}}}+\sum_{{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{disconnected}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\prod_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{t_{k}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq I_{5}+I_{6},

where

\displaystyle I_{5}=\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\sum_{{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{connected}}}\prod_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{t_{k}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}}\right)K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

and

\displaystyle I_{6}=\sum_{{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{disconnected}}}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\prod_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{t_{k}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y).

By Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{connected}}\prod_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{t_{k}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{connected}}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{t_{k}}{p_{0}}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{p_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{connected}}\sum_{k\in I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{p_{0}}.

For any fixed
k=0,\ldots,n
, there exist at most
2^{p_{0}-1}
connected sequences
\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}
such that
k\in I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})
, hence

\displaystyle\sum_{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{connected}}\sum_{k\in I(t_{0},\ldots,t_{n})}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{p_{0}}\leq 2^{p_{0}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{p_{0}}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle I_{5}\leq 2^{p_{0}-1}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{p_{0}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}}\right)K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=2^{p_{0}-1}(I_{51}+I_{52}+I_{53}),

where

\displaystyle I_{51}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{p}}\right)K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

\displaystyle I_{52}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}

|k-l|=1\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{p_{0}}}\left(b_{l}\lvert\phi_{l}(x)-\phi_{l}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}}\right)K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

\displaystyle I_{53}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\cdot\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,l=0\\
|k-l|\geq 2\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(b_{k}\lvert\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k}(y)\rvert\right)^{{p_{0}}}\left(b_{l}\lvert\phi_{l}(x)-\phi_{l}(y)\rvert\right)^{\delta_{0}}\right)K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y).

For

, by Equation (

), we have

\displaystyle I_{51}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}^{p}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\phi_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n}b_{k}^{p}\left(C\int_{B_{k+1}\backslash\overline{B_{k}}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B_{k+1}\backslash\overline{B_{k}}}(f)+\frac{C}{\Upsilon(s_{k+1})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}C\left(\frac{1-b}{b}\right)^{p}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\int_{B_{k+1}\backslash\overline{B_{k}}}\lvert\phi\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)

For

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+C_{5}\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\frac{b^{pk}}{\Upsilon(a^{k}r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

We now consider

. For any fixed

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left(b_{i}\lvert\phi_{i}(x)-\phi_{i}(y)\rvert\right)^{{s_{i}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}\}:\text{disconnected}}\\

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left(b_{i}\lvert\phi_{i}(x)-\phi_{i}(y)\rvert\right)^{{s_{i}}}\left(\sum_{i=k+2}^{n}\left(b_{i}\lvert\phi_{i}(x)-\phi_{i}(y)\rvert\right)\right)^{p_{0}-\sum_{i=0}^{k}s_{i}}.

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left(b_{i}\lvert\phi_{i}(x)-\phi_{i}(y)\rvert\right)^{{s_{i}}}\left(\sum_{i=k+2}^{n}\left(b_{i}\lvert\phi_{i}(x)-\phi_{i}(y)\rvert\right)\right)^{p_{0}-\sum_{i=0}^{k}s_{i}}
