\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}^{{s_{i}}}\left(\sum_{i=k+2}^{n}b_{i}\right)^{p_{0}-\sum_{i=0}^{k}s_{i}}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,s_{k+2})}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{(k-\sum_{i=0}^{k}s_{i})\sum_{i=0}^{k}s_{i}}b^{k(p_{0}-\sum_{i=0}^{k}s_{i})}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(s_{k+2})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=b^{p_{0}k-\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k}s_{i}\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(s_{k+2})}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\lesssim\frac{b^{p_{0}k}}{\Upsilon(a^{k}r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

For fixed
k
, there exist at most
2^{p_{0}-2}
sequences
\{s_{0},\ldots,s_{k}\}
satisfying (
4.8
). Hence, taking the sum over
k
and
\{s_{0},\ldots,s_{k}\}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{6}\leq C_{6}\sum_{k=0}^{n-2}\frac{b^{p_{0}k}}{\Upsilon(a^{k}r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq C_{6}\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\frac{b^{p_{0}k}}{\Upsilon(a^{k}r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

where
C_{6}>0
is some constant. Combining this with Equation (
4.7
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\varphi_{n})\leq I_{5}+I_{6}

\displaystyle\leq 5\cdot 2^{p_{0}-1}C\left(\frac{1-b}{b}\right)^{p}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\lvert\phi\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+(C_{5}+C_{6})\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\frac{b^{p_{0}k}}{\Upsilon(a^{k}r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

where by Equation (
2.1
), we have

\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\frac{b^{p_{0}k}}{\Upsilon(a^{k}r)}\leq\frac{C_{\Upsilon}}{\Upsilon(r)}\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{b^{p_{0}}}{a^{\beta^{(2)}_{\Upsilon}}}\right)^{k}.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, first choose
b\in(0,1)
sufficiently close to 1 so that
5\cdot 2^{p_{0}-1}C\left(\frac{1-b}{b}\right)^{p}<\varepsilon
; second, since

\frac{b^{p_{0}}}{a^{\beta^{(2)}_{\Upsilon}}}\to b^{p_{0}}<1\text{ as }a\uparrow 1,

there exists
a\in(0,1)
, depending only on
b,p_{0},\beta^{(2)}_{\Upsilon}
such that
\frac{b^{p_{0}}}{a^{\beta^{(2)}_{\Upsilon}}}<1
, then
C_{7}=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{b^{p_{0}}}{a^{\beta^{(2)}_{\Upsilon}}}\right)^{k}<+\infty
. In summary, in case
(a)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}(\varphi_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\lvert\phi\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(f)+\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R^{\prime})}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

where
C_{\varepsilon}=C_{\Upsilon}(C_{5}+C_{6})C_{7}
. Compared with case
(a)
, in case
(b)
, the terms
I_{52}
and
I_{53}
do not appear, and in case
(c)
, the term
I_{6}
does not appear; in both cases, the argument still applies.

Finally, if
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
holds, then by the construction of
\phi
as in Equations (
4.4
) and (
4.6
), we can take
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
.
∎

5
Proof of Proposition
2.6

In this section, we give the proof of Proposition
2.6
following the ideas in
[
12
]
. Throughout this section, we always assume that
(\mathcal{E}^{(J)},\mathcal{F}^{(J)})
is a non-local
p
-form associated with
\Upsilon
. By the remark following Proposition
2.6
, we only need to prove the following result.

Proposition 5.1
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
. Then

(1)

\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
is uniformly dense in
C_{c}(X)
.

(2)

\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
is
L^{p}
-dense in
L^{p}(X;m)
.

(3)

\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
is
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}_{1}
-dense in
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
.

The proofs of
(1)
and
(2)
are relatively direct as follows.

Proofs of Proposition
5.1

(1)
and
(2)
.

It is obvious that
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
is a sub-algebra of
C_{c}(X)
. By the Stone-Weierstrass theorem, to show that
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
is uniformly dense in
C_{c}(X)
, we only need to show that
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
separates points and vanishes nowhere. Indeed, for any distinct
x,y\in X
, let
R=d(x,y)\in(0,+\infty)
, by Proposition
4.1
, there exists a cutoff function
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
for
B(x,\frac{1}{4}R)\subseteq B(x,\frac{1}{2}R)
, in particular, we have
\phi(x)=1\neq 0=\phi(y)
, thus we have
(1)
.

For any
u\in L^{p}(X;m)
and any
\varepsilon>0
. First, there exists
v\in C_{c}(X)
such that
\lVert u-v\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}<\varepsilon
. Second, take
x_{0}\in X
and
R>0
such that
\mathrm{supp}(v)\subseteq B(x_{0},R)
. By
(1)
, there exists
w\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
such that
\sup_{X}\lvert v-w\rvert<\frac{\varepsilon}{V(x_{0},2R)^{\frac{1}{p}}}
. By Proposition
4.1
, there exists a cutoff function
\psi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},2R)
, then
\psi w\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
and

\int_{X}\lvert\psi w-v\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\lvert w-v\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m+\int_{B(x_{0},2R)\backslash B(x_{0},R)}\lvert w\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\varepsilon^{p},

hence
\lVert\psi w-u\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}\leq\lVert\psi w-v\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}+\lVert u-v\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}<2\varepsilon
, which gives
(2)
.
∎

We make some preparations in order to prove
(3)
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then

\left\{u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m):\mathrm{supp}(u)\text{ is compact}\right\}

is
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}_{1}
-dense in
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
.

Proof.

For any
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
and any
M\geq 1
, we have
(u\vee(-M))\wedge M\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
and by the dominated convergence theorem, we have
\lim_{M\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(J)}(u-(u\vee(-M))\wedge M)=0
. Hence we only need to show that any
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
can be
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}_{1}
-approximated by functions in
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
with compact support.

Fix arbitrary
x_{0}\in X
. By Proposition
4.1
, there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for any
R>0
, there exists a cutoff function
\phi_{R}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},2R)
such that

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},3R)}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},3R)}(\phi_{R})

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x_{0},2R)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},2R)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(u)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},3R)}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(5.1)

Then
\phi_{R}u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
has compact support. By the dominated convergence theorem, we have
\lim_{R\to+\infty}\lVert u-\phi_{R}u\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}=0
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u-\phi_{R}u)=\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert(1-\phi_{R}(x))u(x)-(1-\phi_{R}(y))u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{R}(x)-\phi_{R}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+2^{p-1}\int_{X}\int_{X}(1-\phi_{R}(y))^{p}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=2^{p-1}\left(I_{1}+I_{2}\right).

For
I_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}=\left(\int_{B(x_{0},3R)}\int_{B(x_{0},3R)}+\int_{B(x_{0},3R)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},3R)}+\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},3R)}\int_{B(x_{0},3R)}\right)\ldots m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I_{11}+I_{12}+I_{13}.

By Equation (
5.1
) and the dominated convergence theorem, we have

\displaystyle I_{11}=\int_{B(x_{0},3R)}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},3R)}(\phi_{R})

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x_{0},2R)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},2R)\backslash\overline{B(x_{0},R)}}(u)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(R)}\int_{X}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\to 0

as
R\to+\infty
. By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle I_{12}=\int_{B(x_{0},2R)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},3R)}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p}\phi_{R}(x)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x_{0},2R)}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,R)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},2R)}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}\int_{X}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\to 0

as
R\to+\infty
, and

\displaystyle I_{13}=\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},3R)}\int_{B(x_{0},2R)}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p}\phi_{R}(y)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},3R)}\lvert u(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,R)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},3R)}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}\int_{X}\lvert u\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\to 0

as
R\to+\infty
. Hence
\lim_{R\to+\infty}I_{1}=0
. By the dominated convergence theorem, we have

I_{2}\leq\int_{X}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\to 0

as
R\to+\infty
. In summary, we have
\lim_{R\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}^{(J)}_{1}(u-\phi_{R}u)=0
.
∎

We have the following partition of unity.

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{cont}}(\Upsilon)
. For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a countable subset
V\subseteq X
satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

\cup_{v\in V}B(v,\varepsilon)=X
.

(b)

For any distinct
v,w\in V
, we have
B(v,\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon)\cap B(w,\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon)=\emptyset
.

Moreover, there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
, independent of
\varepsilon
, and a family of functions
\{\phi_{v}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X):v\in V\}
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

For any
v\in V
, we have
0\leq\phi_{v}\leq 1
in
X
and
\phi_{v}=0
on
X\backslash B(v,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)
.

(2)

\sum_{v\in V}\phi_{v}=1
.

(3)

For any
v\in V
, let

N_{v}=\{w\in V:B(w,3\varepsilon)\cap B(v,3\varepsilon)\neq\emptyset\},

then for any
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}(\phi_{v})\leq C_{1}\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}(f)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,6\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

Proof.

By Zorn’s lemma, there exists a subset
V\subseteq X
satisfying
(a)
and
(b)
. Since
X
is separable, we have
V
is countable. By
VD
, there exists a positive integer
N
depending only on
C_{VD}
such that
\#N_{v}\leq N
for any
v\in V
. By Proposition
4.1
, there exists
C>0
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that for any
v\in V
, there exists a cutoff function
\psi_{v}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
for
B(v,\varepsilon)\subseteq B(v,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)
such that for any
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
, we have

\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}(\psi_{v})\leq\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}(f)+\frac{C}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(5.2)

In any bounded open set, the summation
\sum_{v\in V}\psi_{v}
consists of finitely many non-zero terms and
1\leq\sum_{v\in V}\psi_{v}\leq N
. For any
v\in V
, let

\phi_{v}=\frac{\psi_{v}}{\sum_{w\in V}\psi_{w}},

then
\phi_{v}\in C_{c}(X)
,
(1)
and
(2)
follow directly, in particular, we have

\phi_{v}=\frac{\psi_{v}}{\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\psi_{w}}\text{ in }B(v,3\varepsilon),

and for any
x,y\in B(v,3\varepsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert=\lvert\frac{\psi_{v}(x)}{\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\psi_{w}(x)}-\frac{\psi_{v}(y)}{\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\psi_{w}(y)}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lvert\psi_{v}(x)-\psi_{v}(y)\rvert}{\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\psi_{w}(x)}+\psi_{v}(y)\frac{\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\lvert\psi_{w}(x)-\psi_{w}(y)\rvert}{\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\psi_{w}(x)\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\psi_{w}(y)}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\psi_{v}(x)-\psi_{v}(y)\rvert+\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\lvert\psi_{w}(x)-\psi_{w}(y)\rvert,

which gives

\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}\leq(N+1)^{p-1}\left(\lvert\psi_{v}(x)-\psi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}+\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\lvert\psi_{w}(x)-\psi_{w}(y)\rvert^{p}\right).

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\psi_{v}(x)-\psi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\psi_{w}(x)-\psi_{w}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I+\sum_{w\in N_{v}}I_{w}.

By Equation (
5.2
), we have

I\lesssim\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

For any
w\in N_{v}
, we have

I_{w}=\left(\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}B(v,3\varepsilon)\times B(v,3\varepsilon)\\
d(x,y)<\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon\end{subarray}}+\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}B(v,3\varepsilon)\times B(v,3\varepsilon)\\
d(x,y)\geq\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon\end{subarray}}\right)\ldots m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)=I_{w1}+I_{w2}.

For any
x,y\in B(v,3\varepsilon)
with
d(x,y)<\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon
, we have
\psi_{w}(x)-\psi_{w}(y)\neq 0
only if
x,y\in B(w,\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon)\subseteq B(w,3\varepsilon)
, hence by Equation (
5.2
), we have

\displaystyle I_{w1}\leq\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\psi_{w}(x)-\psi_{w}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle I_{w2}\leq\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

Since
\#N_{v}\leq N
and
B(w,3\varepsilon)\subseteq B(v,6\varepsilon)
for any
w\in N_{v}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\lesssim I+\sum_{w\in N_{v}}I_{w}\lesssim\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,6\varepsilon)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(5.3)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}+2\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\int_{X\backslash B(v,3\varepsilon)}\right)\ldots m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)=J_{1}+2J_{2}.

By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}=\int_{B(v,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)}\int_{X\backslash B(v,3\varepsilon)}\phi_{v}(x)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(v,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,\frac{7}{4}\varepsilon)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)<+\infty.

By taking
f\equiv 1
in Equation (
5.3
), we have
J_{1}<+\infty
. Hence
\phi_{v}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
and
(3)
follows from Equation (
5.3
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.1

(3)
.

By Lemma
5.2
, we only need to show that any
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
with compact support can be
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}_{1}
-approximated by functions in
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, let
V
and
\{\phi_{v}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X):v\in V\}
be given as in Lemma
5.3
. Let

u^{(\varepsilon)}=\sum_{v\in V}u_{B(v,\varepsilon)}\phi_{v},

then
u^{(\varepsilon)}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(X)
is well-defined. Moreover, we have

u-u^{(\varepsilon)}=\sum_{v\in V}(u-u_{B(v,\varepsilon)})\phi_{v}=\sum_{v\in V}u_{v}\phi_{v},

where
u_{v}=u-u_{B(v,\varepsilon)}
. By
VD
, there exists a positive integer
N
depending only on
C_{VD}
such that

\sum_{v\in V}1_{B(v,12\varepsilon)}\leq N1_{\cup_{v\in V}B(v,12\varepsilon)}\leq N,

then
V
can be written as a disjoint union
V=\sqcup_{i=1}^{N}V_{i}
such that, for any
i=1,\ldots,N
, the family
\{B(v,12\varepsilon):v\in V_{i}\}
is pairwise disjoint.

Firstly, we show that
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\lVert u-u^{(\varepsilon)}\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}=0
. Indeed, for any
x\in X
, the summation
\sum_{v\in V}u_{v}(x)\phi_{v}(x)
consists of at most
N
non-zero terms, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\lvert u-u^{(\varepsilon)}\rvert^{p}=\lvert\sum_{v\in V}u_{v}\phi_{v}\rvert^{p}\leq N^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\phi_{v}^{p},

which gives

\int_{X}\lvert u-u^{(\varepsilon)}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq N^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V}\int_{X}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\phi_{v}^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq N^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

By
VD
,

⁢

PI

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m=\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u-u_{B(v,\varepsilon)}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m(B(v,\varepsilon))}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{p}m(B(v,2\varepsilon))}{m(B(v,\varepsilon))}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u-u_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\lesssim\Upsilon(\varepsilon)\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(u).

(5.4)

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{X}\lvert u-u^{(\varepsilon)}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\lesssim\Upsilon(\varepsilon)\sum_{v\in V}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(u)

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon(\varepsilon)\int_{X}\left(\sum_{v\in V}1_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(u)\leq N\Upsilon(\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u)\to 0

as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
.

Secondly, we show that
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u-u^{(\varepsilon)})=0
. By Minkowski’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u-u^{(\varepsilon)})^{\frac{1}{p}}=\left(\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert\sum_{v\in V}\left(u_{v}(x)\phi_{v}(x)-u_{v}(y)\phi_{v}(y)\right)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\int_{X}\int_{X}\left(\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\lvert u_{v}(x)\phi_{v}(x)-u_{v}(y)\phi_{v}(y)\rvert\right)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

For any
x,y\in X
, we have
u_{v}(x)\phi_{v}(x)-u_{v}(y)\phi_{v}(y)\neq 0
only if
x\in B(v,2\varepsilon)
or
y\in B(v,2\varepsilon)
; moreover, noting that
B(v,2\varepsilon)\cap B(w,2\varepsilon)=\emptyset
for any distinct
v,w\in V_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\lvert u_{v}(x)\phi_{v}(x)-u_{v}(y)\phi_{v}(y)\rvert\right)^{p}\leq\left(\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\lvert\ldots\rvert 1_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(x)+\sum_{{v\in V_{i}}}\lvert\ldots\rvert 1_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(y)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left(\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\lvert\ldots\rvert 1_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(x)\right)^{p}+2^{p-1}\left(\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\lvert\ldots\rvert 1_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(y)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=2^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\lvert\ldots\rvert^{p}1_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(x)+2^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\lvert\ldots\rvert^{p}1_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(y).

By symmetry, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u-u^{(\varepsilon)})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{X}\lvert u_{v}(x)\phi_{v}(x)-u_{v}(y)\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(I_{i}^{(\varepsilon)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Recall that
N
is independent of
\varepsilon
, it remains to show that
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}I_{i}^{(\varepsilon)}=0
for any
i=1,\ldots,N
. Noting that

\displaystyle I_{i}^{(\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{X}\lvert u_{v}(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi_{v}(x)-\phi_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+2^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{X}\lvert u_{v}(x)-u_{v}(y)\rvert^{p}\phi_{v}(y)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=2^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{i}}I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i1v}+2^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{i}}I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i2v}.

For
I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i1v}
, we have

\displaystyle I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i1v}=\left(\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}+\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{X\backslash B(v,3\varepsilon)}\right)\ldots m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)=J_{1}+J_{2}.

By Lemma
5.3

(3)
, we have

\displaystyle J_{1}\leq\int_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,3\varepsilon)}(\phi_{v})

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}(u_{v})+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,6\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=C_{1}\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}(u)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,6\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

where
N_{v}=\{w\in V:B(w,3\varepsilon)\cap B(v,3\varepsilon)\neq\emptyset\}
. For any
w\in N_{v}
, we have
B(w,3\varepsilon)\subseteq B(v,9\varepsilon)
, hence

\displaystyle\sum_{w\in N_{v}}\int_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}(u)\leq\int_{X}\left(\sum_{w\in N_{v}}1_{B(w,3\varepsilon)}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}(u)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{X}N1_{\cup_{{w\in N_{v}}}B(w,3\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}(u)\leq N\int_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}(u).

Similar to Equation (
5.4
), we also have

\displaystyle\int_{B(v,6\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\lesssim\Upsilon(\varepsilon)\int_{B(v,6\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,6\varepsilon)}(u).

Hence

\displaystyle J_{1}\lesssim\int_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}(u).

By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}=\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{X\backslash B(v,3\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}(x)\rvert^{p}\phi_{v}(x)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,\varepsilon)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{v}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\lesssim\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(u),

where the last inequality follows from Equation (
5.4
). Hence

I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i1v}=J_{1}+J_{2}\lesssim\int_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}(u).

For
I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i2v}
, we have

I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i2v}=\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}\phi_{v}(y)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\leq\int_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,2\varepsilon)}(u).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle I_{i}^{(\varepsilon)}\leq 2^{p-1}\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\left(I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i1v}+I^{(\varepsilon)}_{i2v}\right)\lesssim\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\int_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}(u)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in V_{i}}\int_{B(v,9\varepsilon)}\left(\int_{B(x,18\varepsilon)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}\int_{X}\left(\int_{B(x,18\varepsilon)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)\overset{(\dagger)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\to$}}0

as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, where
(\star)
follows from the fact that the family
\{B(v,9\varepsilon):v\in V_{i}\}
is pairwise disjoint, and
(\dagger)
follows from the dominated convergence theorem.
∎

6
Proof of Theorem
2.8

In this section, we give the proof of Theorem
2.8
. Recall that
(X,d,m)
is a metric measure space. For any doubling function
\Psi
satisfying Equation (
2.1
), any open subset
U\subseteq X
, any
u\in L^{p}(X;m)
, and any
r>0
, we define

E_{\Psi,U}(u,r)=\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\int_{U}\left(\frac{1}{V(x,r)}\int_{B(x,r)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x),

and

U(r)=\bigcup_{x\in U}B(x,r).

The following characterization of “
\iint\ldots
” in terms of “
\sum E\ldots
” plays an important role in our proof.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume
VD
. Let
\Upsilon
be a doubling function satisfying Equation (
2.1
) with constants
C_{\Upsilon},\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon},\beta^{(2)}_{\Upsilon}
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any
u\in L^{p}(X;m)
, for any non-empty bounded open subset
U\subseteq X
, for any
D\geq\mathrm{diam}(U)
and any
R>0
, we have

\int_{U}\int_{U}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\leq C\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}E_{\Upsilon,U}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}}D),

(6.1)

\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}E_{\Upsilon,U}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)\leq C\int_{U(R)}\int_{U(R)}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

(6.2)

and

\frac{1}{C}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}E_{\Upsilon,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}})\leq\int_{X}\int_{X}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\leq{C}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}E_{\Upsilon,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}}).

(6.3)

Proof.

The proof follows a standard annulus decomposition argument; see also
[
23
, Theorem 5.2]
. Firstly, we have

\displaystyle\int_{U}\int_{U}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{U}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\int_{B(x,\frac{1}{2^{n}}D)\backslash B(x,\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}D)}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{U}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}D)\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}D)}\int_{B(x,\frac{1}{2^{n}}D)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}E_{\Upsilon,U}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}}D),

where
C_{1}=C_{\Upsilon}C_{VD}
, which gives Equation (
6.1
). Secondly, noting that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}E_{\Upsilon,U}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)}\int_{U}\left(\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)}\sum_{k=n}^{+\infty}\int_{B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)\backslash B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k+1}}R)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\int_{U}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{k}\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)}\right)\left(\int_{B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)\backslash B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k+1}}R)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x),

where by Equation (
2.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{k}\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)}\leq\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Upsilon}\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)}\sum_{n=0}^{k}2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}(n-k)}\leq\frac{2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}C_{\Upsilon}}{2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}-1}\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)}.

Let
C_{2}=\frac{2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}C_{\Upsilon}}{2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}-1}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}E_{\Upsilon,U}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}}R)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\int_{U}\left(\frac{1}{V(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)}\int_{B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)\backslash B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k+1}}R)}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\int_{U}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\int_{B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k}}R)\backslash B(x,\frac{1}{2^{k+1}}R)}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=C_{2}\int_{U}\left(\int_{B(x,R)}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\int_{U(R)}\int_{U(R)}\frac{\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p}}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

which gives Equation (
6.2
). Finally, by replacing
U
with
X
,
\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}
with
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
,
\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}
with
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
,
\sum_{n=0}^{k}
with
\sum_{\mathbb{Z}\ni n\leq k}
, and by letting
D=1
,
R=1
in the above estimates, we have Equation (
6.3
).
∎

For a strongly local regular
p
-energy
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
, we have the relation between
\Gamma^{(L)}
and
E
as follows.

Lemma 6.2

(
[
43
, Proposition 3.5, Equation (1.18)]
)
.

Assume
VD
. Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
be a strongly local regular
p
-energy satisfying

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any open subset
U\subseteq X
, for any
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
, for any
r>0
, we have

E_{\Psi,U}(u,r)\leq C\Gamma^{(L)}(u)\left(U(2A_{PI}r)\right),

where
A_{PI}\geq 1
is the constant in

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

.

We have the relation between the domains of
p
-forms as follows.

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume
VD
.

(1)

Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
be a strongly local regular
p
-energy satisfying

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

. Let
\Upsilon
be a doubling function satisfying

⁢
SUG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

and
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
a non-local
p
-form associated with
\Upsilon
. Then
\mathcal{F}^{(L)}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)}
.

(2)

Let
\Psi,\Upsilon
be doubling functions satisfying

⁢
UG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

and
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Psi)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Psi)})
,
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
non-local
p
-forms associated with
\Psi,\Upsilon
, respectively. Then
\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Psi)}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)}
.

Proof.

For any
u\in L^{p}(X;m)
, by Equation (
6.3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)}(u)\lesssim\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}E_{\Upsilon,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}})=\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}+\sum_{n<0}\right)E_{\Upsilon,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}})=I_{1}+I_{2}.

For any
r>0
, by
VD
, we have

E_{\Upsilon,X}(u,r)\leq\frac{2^{p-1}(C_{VD}+1)}{\Upsilon(r)}\lVert u\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}^{p},

see also
[
8
, Lemma 3.1]
, hence

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}^{p}\sum_{n<0}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}})}\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(1)}\lVert u\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)}^{p}\sum_{n<0}2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}n}<+\infty.

(1)
For any
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
, for any
n\geq 0
, by Lemma
6.2
, we have

E_{\Psi,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}})\lesssim\Gamma^{(L)}(u)(X)=\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(u),

hence by

⁢
SUG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

, we have

I_{1}=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{\Psi(\frac{1}{2^{n}})}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}})}E_{\Psi,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}})\lesssim\frac{\Psi(1)}{\Upsilon(1)}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(u)\sum_{n\geq 0}\Theta(\frac{1}{2^{n}})<+\infty,

which gives
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)}
.

(2)
For any
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Psi)}
, by

⁢
UG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

and Equation (
6.3
), we have

I_{1}=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{\Psi(\frac{1}{2^{n}})}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}})}E_{\Psi,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}})\lesssim\frac{\Psi(1)}{\Upsilon(1)}\sum_{n\geq 0}E_{\Psi,X}(u,\frac{1}{2^{n}})\lesssim\frac{\Psi(1)}{\Upsilon(1)}\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Psi)}(u)<+\infty,

which gives
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)}
.
∎

We give the proof of Theorem
2.8
as follows.

Proof of Theorem
2.8
.

For notational convenience, we write
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}
,
\Gamma^{(\bullet)}
, … to denote
\mathcal{E}^{(L)}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
,
\Gamma^{(L)}
, … in
(a)
, and
\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Psi)}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Psi)}
,
\Gamma^{(J,\Psi)}
, … associated with
\Psi
in
(b)
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
,
A=A_{E}>1
be the constants in
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
. By replacing
\delta
with
\min\{\delta,\frac{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}{2p}\}
, we may assume that
\delta\leq\frac{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}{2p}<\frac{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}{p}
.

For any ball
B(x_{0},r)
, let
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}
be a cutoff function for
B(x_{0},r)\subseteq B(x_{0},Ar)
given by
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
. By Lemma
6.3
, we have
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)}
. To prove that
\phi
satisfies
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
, since
\text{CE}^{(J)}(\Upsilon)\text{-}1
follows trivially from
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)\text{-}1
, it remains to prove
\text{CE}^{(J)}(\Upsilon)\text{-}2
.

For any
x\in X
,
s>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J,\Upsilon)}_{X}(\phi)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{B(x,2s)}\int_{B(x,2s)}+\int_{B(x,s)}\int_{X\backslash B(x,2s)}\right)\frac{\lvert\phi(y)-\phi(z)\rvert^{p}}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

(6.4)

By
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)\text{-}1
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\int_{B(x,s)}\int_{X\backslash B(y,s)}\left(\frac{d(y,z)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\frac{1}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x,s)}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\int_{B(y,2^{n+1}s)\backslash B(y,2^{n}s)}\left(\frac{d(y,z)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\frac{1}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}z)\right)m(\mathrm{d}y).

Since

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\int_{B(y,2^{n+1}s)\backslash B(y,2^{n}s)}\left(\frac{d(y,z)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\frac{1}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{2^{n+1}s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\frac{V(y,2^{n+1}s)}{V(y,2^{n}s)\Upsilon(2^{n}s)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(s)}\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{2^{n+1}s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\frac{1}{2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}n}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\diamond)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\asymp$}}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(s)}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\leq\frac{1}{\Upsilon(s)}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta},

where
(\diamond)
follows from the fact that
p\delta<\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}
, we have

I_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}.

(6.5)

By Equation (
6.1
), we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}E_{\Upsilon,B(x,2s)}(\phi,\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\Psi(\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))}E_{\Psi,B(x,2s)}(\phi,\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s)).

If
s\leq Ar
, then for
(a)
, for any
n\geq 0
, by Lemma
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle E_{\Psi,B(x,2s)}(\phi,\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))\lesssim\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\left(B(x,2s+2A_{PI}\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))\right)\leq\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)(B(x,16A_{PI}s))

\displaystyle\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\lesssim$}}\left(\frac{16A_{PI}s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,16A_{PI}s)}{\Psi((16A_{PI}s)\wedge r)}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)},

where
(\star)
follows from
\text{CE}^{(L)}(\Psi)\text{-}2
. By

⁢
SUG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)}\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\Psi(\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)}\frac{\Psi(s)}{\Upsilon(s)}\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\Theta\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}.

(6.6)

For
(b)
, by

⁢
UG

(
Υ
,
Ψ
)

and Equation (
6.2
), we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\Psi(\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))}{\Upsilon(\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))}E_{\Psi,B(x,2s)}(\phi,\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))\lesssim\frac{\Psi(4s)}{\Upsilon(4s)}\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}E_{\Psi,B(x,2s)}(\phi,\frac{1}{2^{n}}(4s))

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\Psi(s)}{\Upsilon(s)}\int_{B(x,6s)}\int_{B(x,6s)}\frac{\lvert\phi(y)-\phi(z)\rvert^{p}}{V(y,d(y,z))\Psi(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z).

By
\text{CE}^{(J)}(\Psi)\text{-}2
, we have

I_{1}\lesssim\frac{\Psi(s)}{\Upsilon(s)}\int_{B(x,6s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J,\Psi)}_{X}(\phi)\lesssim\frac{\Psi(s)}{\Upsilon(s)}\left(\frac{6s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,6s)}{\Psi((6s)\wedge r)}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}.

(6.7)

Combining Equation (
6.6
) for
(a)
and Equation (
6.7
) for
(b)
, we have if
s\leq Ar
, then

I_{1}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}.

(6.8)

If
s>Ar
, then

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq\int_{X}\int_{X}\frac{\lvert\phi(y)-\phi(z)\rvert^{p}}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{B(x_{0},2Ar)}\int_{B(x_{0},2Ar)}+\int_{B(x_{0},2Ar)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},2Ar)}+\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},2Ar)}\int_{B(x_{0},2Ar)}\right)\ldots\mathrm{d}m\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=I_{11}+I_{12}+I_{13}.

Taking
x=x_{0}
,
s=Ar
in Equation (
6.8
), we have

I_{11}\lesssim\frac{V(x_{0},r)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

Since

\displaystyle I_{12}=\int_{B(x_{0},Ar)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},2Ar)}\frac{\phi(y)^{p}}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x_{0},Ar)}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(y,Ar)}\frac{1}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}z)\right)m(\mathrm{d}y)\lesssim\frac{V(x_{0},r)}{\Upsilon(r)},

and similarly

I_{13}\lesssim\frac{V(x_{0},r)}{\Upsilon(r)},

we have

I_{1}\leq I_{11}+I_{12}+I_{13}\lesssim\frac{V(x_{0},r)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

If
d(x_{0},x)<3s
, then

I_{1}\lesssim\frac{V(x_{0},r)}{\Upsilon(r)}\leq\frac{V(x,d(x_{0},x)+r)}{\Upsilon(r)}\leq\frac{V(x,3s+\frac{1}{A}s)}{\Upsilon(r)}\lesssim\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

If
d(x_{0},x)\geq 3s
, then
B(x_{0},Ar)\cap B(x,2s)=\emptyset
, hence

I_{1}=\int_{B(x,2s)}\int_{B(x,2s)}\frac{\lvert\phi(y)-\phi(z)\rvert^{p}}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)=0\leq\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

Therefore, if
s>Ar
, then

I_{1}\lesssim\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

Combining this with Equation (
6.8
), we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\lesssim\begin{cases}\left(\frac{s}{r}\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}&\text{if }s\leq Ar,\\
\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(r)}&\text{if }s>Ar,\end{cases}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s\wedge r)}.

Together with Equations (
6.4
) and (
6.5
), we have

\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma_{X}^{(J,\Upsilon)}(\phi)\lesssim I_{1}+I_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s\wedge r)},

which is
\text{CE}^{(J)}(\Upsilon)\text{-}2
. In summary, we have
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
is a non-local
p
-form satisfying
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
. By Proposition
2.6
, we have
(\mathcal{E}^{(J,\Upsilon)},\mathcal{F}^{(J,\Upsilon)})
is a regular
p
-energy.
∎

7
Proof of “
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}\Rightarrow\text{CS}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}
” in Theorems
2.1
and
2.2

For notational convenience, we write
\mathcal{E}^{(\bullet)}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}
,
\Gamma^{(\bullet)}
, … to denote
\mathcal{E}^{(L)}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
,
\Gamma^{(L)}
, … in Theorem
2.1
, and
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
,
\Gamma^{(J)}
, … in Theorem
2.2
. We write
\bullet=L
to mean the setting of Theorem
2.1
, and
\bullet=J
to mean the setting of Theorem
2.2
. We prove these results for the same doubling function
\Psi
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
,
A_{E}>1
be the constants in
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}(\Psi)
. Let
A=8A_{E}A_{PI}
, where
A_{PI}\geq 1
is the constant in

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

for
\bullet=L
, and
A_{PI}=1
as in
\text{PI}^{(J)}(\Psi)
for
\bullet=J
.

For any ball
B(x_{0},R)
, let
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(\bullet)}
be a cutoff function for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},A_{E}R)
given by
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}_{\square}(\Psi)
. For
\bullet=L
, take any
f\in{\mathcal{F}}^{(L)}\cap C_{c}(X)
; and for
\bullet=J
, take any
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
. Write

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)=\left(\int_{B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}+\int_{B(x_{0},AR)\backslash B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\right)\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)=I_{1}+I_{2}.

For
I_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\lesssim\int_{B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\lvert f-f_{B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)+\lvert f_{B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\rvert^{p}\int_{B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)

\displaystyle=I_{11}+I_{12}.

By
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)\text{-}2
and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle I_{12}\lesssim\frac{V(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}{\Psi(R)}\frac{1}{V(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\int_{B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

For any
n\geq 0
, let
\varepsilon_{n}=\frac{1}{2^{n}}(2A_{E}R)
and
V_{n}
an
\varepsilon_{n}
-net of
X
with
x_{0}\in V_{n}
. For any Lebesgue point
x\in B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)
of
f
(with respect to the measure
m
), for any
n\geq 0
, there exists
y_{n}\in V_{n}
such that
d(x,y_{n})<\varepsilon_{n}
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
y_{0}=x_{0}
. Since
d(y_{n},y_{n+1})<\varepsilon_{n}+\varepsilon_{n+1}
, we have
B(y_{n+1},\varepsilon_{n+1})\subseteq B(y_{n},2\varepsilon_{n})
. By
VD
,
\text{PI}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)
and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\lvert f_{B(y_{n},\varepsilon_{n})}-f_{B(y_{n+1},\varepsilon_{n+1})}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{V(y_{n},2\varepsilon_{n})^{2}}\int_{B(y_{n},2\varepsilon_{n})}\int_{B(y_{n},2\varepsilon_{n})}\lvert f(y)-f(z)\rvert m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{V(y_{n},2\varepsilon_{n})^{2}}\int_{B(y_{n},2\varepsilon_{n})}\int_{B(y_{n},2\varepsilon_{n})}\lvert f(y)-f(z)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\Psi(\varepsilon_{n})}{V(y_{n},\varepsilon_{n})}\int_{B(y_{n},2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(y_{n},2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}(f)\leq\frac{\Psi(\varepsilon_{n})}{V(y_{n},\varepsilon_{n})}\int_{B(y_{n},2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f).

Taking any fixed
\alpha\in(0,\frac{\delta}{p})
, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\lvert f(x)-f_{B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\rvert^{p}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\lvert f_{B(y_{n},\varepsilon_{n})}-f_{B(y_{0},\varepsilon_{0})}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}2^{\alpha n}\lvert f_{B(y_{n},\varepsilon_{n})}-f_{B(y_{n+1},\varepsilon_{n+1})}\rvert\cdot 2^{-\alpha n}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}2^{p\alpha n}\lvert f_{B(y_{n},\varepsilon_{n})}-f_{B(y_{n+1},\varepsilon_{n+1})}\rvert^{p}\right)\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}2^{-\alpha\frac{p}{p-1}n}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}2^{p\alpha n}\frac{\Psi(\varepsilon_{n})}{V(y_{n},\varepsilon_{n})}\int_{B(y_{n},2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\sum_{y\in V_{n}\cap B(x_{0},4A_{E}R)}2^{p\alpha n}\frac{\Psi(\varepsilon_{n})}{V(y,\varepsilon_{n})}\left(\int_{B(y,2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f)\right)1_{B(y,\varepsilon_{n})}(x).

For
\bullet=L
, since
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}\cap C_{c}(X)
, it holds for
any

x\in B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)
; for
\bullet=J
, it holds for
m
-a.e.

x\in B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)
, since
\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(\phi)
is absolutely continuous with respect to
m
, it also holds for
\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(\phi)
-a.e.
x\in B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)
. In summary, the above inequality holds for
\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)
-a.e.
x\in B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)
.

By
\text{CE}^{(\bullet)}(\Psi)\text{-}2
, we have

\displaystyle I_{11}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\sum_{y\in V_{n}\cap B(x_{0},4A_{E}R)}2^{p\alpha n}\frac{\Psi(\varepsilon_{n})}{V(y,\varepsilon_{n})}\left(\int_{B(y,2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f)\right)\int_{B(y,\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\sum_{y\in V_{n}\cap B(x_{0},4A_{E}R)}2^{p\alpha n}\frac{\Psi(\varepsilon_{n})}{V(y,\varepsilon_{n})}\left(\int_{B(y,2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f)\right)\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{R}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(y,\varepsilon_{n})}{\Psi(\varepsilon_{n}\wedge R)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}2^{(p\alpha-\delta)n}\int_{X}\left(\sum_{y\in V_{n}\cap B(x_{0},4A_{E}R)}1_{B(y,2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f),

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\varepsilon_{n}\wedge R\asymp\varepsilon_{n}
. By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
M
depending only on
A_{E},A_{PI},C_{VD}
such that

\sum_{y\in V_{n}\cap B(x_{0},4A_{E}R)}1_{B(y,2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\leq M1_{\cup_{{y\in V_{n}\cap B(x_{0},4A_{E}R)}}B(y,2A_{PI}\varepsilon_{n})}\leq M1_{B(x_{0},AR)},

which gives

\displaystyle I_{11}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}2^{(p\alpha-\delta)n}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f)\lesssim\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f),

where the second inequality follows from the fact that
\alpha<\frac{\delta}{p}
. Hence

I_{1}\lesssim I_{11}+I_{12}\lesssim\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

For
I_{2}
, when
\bullet=L
, the strongly local property implies that
I_{2}=0
. When
\bullet=J
, by
\text{T}^{(J)}(\Psi)
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}=\int_{B(x_{0},AR)\backslash B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{0},AR)\backslash B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{B(x_{0},A_{E}R)}\phi(y)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x_{0},AR)\backslash B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,A_{E}R)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)\backslash B(x_{0},2A_{E}R)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

In summary, we have

\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{X}(\phi)=I_{1}+I_{2}\lesssim\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(\bullet)}_{B(x_{0},AR)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

(7.1)

For
\bullet=L
, Equation (
7.1
) holds for any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}\cap C_{c}(X)
, then for any
f\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
, by
[
48
, Proposition 8.5, Proposition 8.12]
, there exists
\{f_{n}\}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{(L)}\cap C_{c}(X)
such that
\{f_{n}\}
is
\mathcal{E}^{(L)}_{1}
-convergent to
f
and
\{f_{n}\}
converges to
\widetilde{f}
q.e. on
X
, which is also
\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)
-a.e. on
X
, then by Fatou’s lemma, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert\widetilde{f}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\leq\varliminf_{n\to+\infty}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f_{n}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varliminf_{n\to+\infty}\left(\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(f_{n})+\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f_{n}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(f)+\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{B(x_{0},AR)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m.

Noting that
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
is also a cutoff function for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},AR)
, we conclude that
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\square}(\Psi)
holds with
A_{S}=A
therein.

For
\bullet=J
, Equation (
7.1
) holds for any
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
, hence by Lemma
11.8
, we have
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\square}(\Psi)
holds with
A_{1}=A_{E}
and
A_{2}=A
therein.
\square

8
Proof of “
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}\Rightarrow\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}
” in Theorem
2.1

Throughout this section, we always assume that
(\mathcal{E}^{(L)},\mathcal{F}^{(L)})
is a strongly local regular
p
-energy. The main results of this section are the following interior and boundary oscillation inequalities, from which we give the proof of “
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
\Rightarrow
\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
” in Theorem
2.1
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume
VD
,

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
. Then there exist
\delta\in(0,1)
,
C>0
such that for any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, let
f\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)
satisfy
\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)}\leq 1
, let
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
be bounded in
\Omega
and satisfy
-\Delta_{p}^{(L)}u=f
in
\Omega
, then for any
x_{0}\in\Omega
and any
R,r>0
with
r\leq R
and
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq\Omega
, we have

\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},r)}u\leq C\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\delta}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

In particular, we have
u\in C(\Omega)
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Assume
VD
,

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
. Let
c\in(0,1)
. Then there exist
\delta\in(0,1)
,
C>0
such that for any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, let
f\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)
satisfy
\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)}\leq 1
, let
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(\Omega)
be non-negative bounded in
\Omega
and satisfy
-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}u=f
in
\Omega
, let
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
satisfy
X\backslash\Omega
has a
(c,r_{0})
-corkscrew at
x_{0}
for some
r_{0}>0
, then for any
R,r\in(0,r_{0})
with
r\leq R
, we have

\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},r)\cap\Omega}u\leq C\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\delta}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)\cap\Omega}u+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

In particular, we have
\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{{B(x_{0},r)\cap\Omega}}u=0
.

The proofs rely on the following interior and boundary weak Harnack inequalities. We will follow the classical Moser iteration technique as in
[
22
, Theorems 8.18 and 8.26]
, while
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
will provide the necessary cutoff functions.

Proposition 8.3
.

Assume
VD
,

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
. Then there exist
q>0
,
A>1
,
C>1
such that the following holds. For any
x_{0}\in X
and any
R>0
, let
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
be non-negative bounded in
B(x_{0},AR)
and satisfy
-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}u\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},AR)
, then we have

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}u^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}u+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

Proposition 8.4
.

Assume
VD
,

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
. Then there exist
q>0
,
A>1
,
C>1
such that the following holds. For any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, any
x_{0}\in X
, and any
R>0
, let
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
be non-negative bounded in
B(x_{0},AR)
and satisfy
\widetilde{u}=M
q.e. on
B(x_{0},AR)\backslash\Omega
, for some constant
M\in[0,+\infty)
, and
-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}u\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},AR)\cap\Omega
, then we have

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left(u\wedge M\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}\left(u\wedge M\right)+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

It suffices to prove Proposition
8.4
. Indeed, assuming Proposition
8.4
, by taking
\Omega=B(x_{0},AR)
and
M=\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},AR);m)}<+\infty
, and noting that
u\wedge M=u
in
B(x_{0},AR)
, we obtain Proposition
8.3
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Assume
VD
,

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
. Then for any
q>0
, there exists
C>1
such that the following holds. For any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, any
x_{0}\in X
, and any
R_{0}>0
, let
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
be non-negative bounded in
B(x_{0},R_{0})
and satisfy
\widetilde{u}=M
q.e. on
B(x_{0},R_{0})\backslash\Omega
, for some constant
M\in[0,+\infty)
, and
-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}u\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},R_{0})\cap\Omega
, then for any
R\in(0,R_{0})
, we have

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}(u\wedge M)+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\geq\frac{1}{C}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{-q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{-\frac{1}{q}}.

Proof.

For notational convenience, we may assume that all functions in
\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
are quasi-continuous. If
M=0
, then the result is trivial, hence we may assume that
M>0
. For any
R,r>0
with
r\leq R
and
R+r<R_{0}
, for any
K\geq\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
, let
\overline{u}=u\wedge M+K
, and
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
a cutoff function for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},R+r)
to be chosen later. For any
\alpha<-(p-1)
, let

v=\phi^{p}\left(\overline{u}^{\alpha}-(M+K)^{\alpha}\right)=\phi^{p}\left((u\wedge M+K)^{\alpha}-(M+K)^{\alpha}\right),

then
v\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(B(x_{0},R+r)\cap\Omega)
is non-negative bounded, hence

\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(u;v)\geq-\int_{X}v\mathrm{d}m\geq-\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m.

(8.1)

On the other hand

\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(u;v)=\alpha\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})+p\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p-1}\left(\overline{u}^{\alpha}-(M+K)^{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u};\phi),

(8.2)

where

\displaystyle p\lvert\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p-1}\left(\overline{u}^{\alpha}-(M+K)^{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u};\phi)\rvert

\displaystyle=p\lvert\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p-1}\overline{u}^{(\alpha-1)\frac{p-1}{p}}\overline{u}^{-(\alpha-1)\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\overline{u}^{\alpha}-(M+K)^{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u};\phi)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq p\left(\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\lvert\alpha\rvert\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})+\frac{C_{1}}{\lvert\alpha\rvert^{p-1}}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi),

(8.3)

here the last inequality follows from Young’s inequality, and
C_{1}>0
depends only on
p
. By the self-improvement property of
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
(see
[
46
, Proposition 3.1]
), for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
C_{\varepsilon}>0
and a cutoff function
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
for
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq B(x_{0},R+r)
such that

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}\left(\overline{u}^{\frac{\alpha+p-1}{p}}\right)+\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{p}\right)^{p}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})+\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m.

(8.4)

Combining Equations (
8.1
)–(
8.4
), we have

\displaystyle\lvert\alpha\rvert\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{4}+\varepsilon C_{1}C_{2}\right)\lvert\alpha\rvert\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{\varepsilon}}{\lvert\alpha\rvert^{p-1}}+1\right)\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m,

where
C_{2}=\sup_{\alpha<-(p-1)}\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{p\lvert\alpha\rvert}\right)^{p}\in(0,+\infty)
depends only on
p
. Let
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{4C_{1}C_{2}}
, then

\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})\leq\frac{2}{\lvert\alpha\rvert^{p}}\left(\lvert\alpha\rvert^{p-1}+C_{1}C_{\varepsilon}\right)\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m,

and

\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\leq\left(2\varepsilon C_{2}\left(\lvert\alpha\rvert^{p-1}+C_{1}C_{\varepsilon}\right)+C_{\varepsilon}\right)\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m,

hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi\overline{u}^{\frac{\alpha+p-1}{p}})

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left(\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{p}\right)^{p}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{\alpha-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})+\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{{\alpha+p-1}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{3}\lvert\alpha\rvert^{p-1}}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m,

for any
\alpha<-(p-1)
, where
C_{3}>0
depends only on
p,\varepsilon,C_{1},C_{2},C_{\varepsilon}
. Since
\phi\overline{u}^{\frac{\alpha+p-1}{p}}\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(B(x_{0},R+r))
, by Proposition
3.11
, there exist
\kappa>1
,
C_{4}>0
such that

\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi\overline{u}^{\frac{\alpha+p-1}{p}})\geq\frac{1}{C_{4}}\frac{V(x_{0},R+r)^{\frac{\kappa-1}{\kappa}}}{\Psi(R+r)}\left(\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}(\phi\overline{u}^{\frac{\alpha+p-1}{p}})^{p\kappa}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}({u}\wedge M+K)^{\kappa(\alpha+p-1)}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}C_{4}C_{VD}^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\lvert\alpha\rvert^{p-1}\frac{\Psi(R+r)}{\Psi(r)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}({u}\wedge M+K)^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m,

(8.5)

for any
\alpha<-(p-1)
,
r\leq R
,
K\geq\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
. For any
q>0
and any
R\in(0,R_{0})
. For any
k\geq 0
, let
R_{k}=(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2^{k+1}})R
and
q_{k}=-q\kappa^{k}
, then
R=R_{0}>R_{1}>\ldots>R_{k}\downarrow\frac{1}{2}R
, and
-q=q_{0}>q_{1}>\ldots>q_{k}\downarrow-\infty
, let

I_{k}=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R_{k})}\left(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{q_{k}}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q_{k}}},

then by Equation (
8.5
), we have

\displaystyle I_{k+1}^{-q}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{3}C_{4}C_{VD}^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\left(q\kappa^{k}+p-1\right)^{p-1}\frac{\Psi(R_{k})}{\Psi(R_{k}-R_{k+1})}\right)^{\frac{1}{\kappa^{k}}}I_{k}^{-q}\leq C_{5}^{\frac{1}{\kappa^{k}}}C_{6}^{\frac{k}{\kappa^{k}}}I_{k}^{-q},

where
C_{5}\geq 1
depends only on
p,q,\kappa,C_{\Psi},C_{VD},C_{3},C_{4}
, and
C_{6}=\kappa^{p-1}C_{\Psi}
. By Lemma
11.1
, we have

I_{k}^{-q}\leq C_{5}^{\frac{\kappa}{\kappa-1}}C_{6}^{\frac{\kappa}{(\kappa-1)^{2}}}I_{0}^{-q},

that is,
I_{k}\geq\frac{1}{C_{7}}I_{0}
, where
C_{7}=\left(C_{5}^{\frac{\kappa}{\kappa-1}}C_{6}^{\frac{\kappa}{(\kappa-1)^{2}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
. Letting
k\to+\infty
, we have

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}(u\wedge M)+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\geq\frac{1}{C_{7}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{-q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{-\frac{1}{q}}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
8.4
.

For notational convenience, we may assume that all functions in
\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
are quasi-continuous. If
M=0
, then the result is trivial, hence we may assume that
M>0
. Let
A_{PI},A_{cap}
be the constants in

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,

⁢

cap

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

≤

, respectively, and
A=48A_{PI}A_{cap}
, then Lemma
8.5
holds with
R_{0}=AR
therein. Let

and

. We claim that

and

\lVert\log\overline{u}\rVert_{\mathrm{BMO}(B(x_{0},12R))}\leq C_{1},

where

depends only on

and the constants in

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

,

⁢

(

)

(
Ψ
)

≤

. Indeed, for any ball

with radius

, we have
r<24R
and
A_{PI}A_{cap}B\subseteq B(x_{0},AR)
. Since
\log\frac{\overline{u}}{K}\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
, by

⁢

PI

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B}\lvert\log\overline{u}-\left(\log\overline{u}\right)_{B}\rvert\mathrm{d}m\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B}\lvert\log\frac{\overline{u}}{K}-\left(\log\frac{\overline{u}}{K}\right)_{B}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{m(B)}\int_{A_{PI}B}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}\left(\log\frac{\overline{u}}{K}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{m(B)}\int_{A_{PI}B}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\log\overline{u})\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(8.6)

By

⁢

cap

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

≤

, there exists a cutoff function
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
for
A_{PI}B\subseteq A_{PI}A_{cap}B
such that

\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi)\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Psi(r)},

(8.7)

hence

\displaystyle\int_{A_{PI}B}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\log\overline{u})\leq\int_{X}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{-p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})=\int_{X}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{-p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u;\overline{u})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{p-1}\int_{X}\phi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}\left(u;\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{p-1}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(L)}\left(u;\phi^{p}\left(\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt\left.-p\int_{X}\phi^{p-1}\left(\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u};\phi)\right).

(8.8)

Since
\phi^{p}\left(\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(A_{PI}A_{cap}B\cap\Omega)
is non-negative, by assumption, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(L)}\left(u;\phi^{p}\left(\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)\right)\geq-\int_{X}\phi^{p}\left(\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{\Psi(R)}\int_{X}\phi^{p}\mathrm{d}m\geq-\frac{m(A_{PI}A_{cap}B)}{\Psi(R)}\geq-C_{\Psi}^{5}\frac{m(A_{PI}A_{cap}B)}{\Psi(r)},

(8.9)

where the last inequality follows from Equation (
2.1
) and the fact that
r<24R
. Moreover

\displaystyle\frac{p}{p-1}\lvert\int_{X}\phi^{p-1}\left(\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u};\phi)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{p-1}\left(\int_{X}\phi^{p}\left(\overline{u}^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{X}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{p-1}\left(\int_{X}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{-p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{X}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{-p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})+C_{2}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi),

where the last inequality follows from Young’s inequality, and
C_{2}>0
depends only on
p
. Combining this with Equations (
8.8
) and (
8.9
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{X}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{-p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})\leq\frac{2C_{\Psi}^{5}}{p-1}\frac{m(A_{PI}A_{cap}B)}{\Psi(r)}+2C_{2}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi).

Then by Equation (
8.7
),
VD
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A_{PI}B}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\log\overline{u})\leq\int_{X}\phi^{p}\overline{u}^{-p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\overline{u})\leq\frac{2C_{\Psi}^{5}}{p-1}\frac{m(A_{PI}A_{cap}B)}{\Psi(r)}+2C_{2}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\phi)\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Psi(r)}.

By Equation (
8.6
), we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B}\lvert\log\overline{u}-\left(\log\overline{u}\right)_{B}\rvert\mathrm{d}m\leq C_{1},

where
C_{1}>0
is some constant. By Lemma
3.3
, there exist
C_{3},C_{4}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\exp\left(\frac{C_{4}}{2C_{1}}\log(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}})\right)\mathrm{d}m\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{4}}{2C_{1}}\log(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}})\right)\mathrm{d}m\right)

\displaystyle\leq(C_{3}+1)^{2}.

Let
q=\frac{C_{4}}{2C_{1}}
, then

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}})^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}})^{-q}\mathrm{d}m\right)\leq(C_{3}+1)^{2}.

Combining this with Lemma
8.5
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}(u\wedge M)+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}(u\wedge M+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}})^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\geq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}(u\wedge M)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

∎

We give the proofs of Propositions
8.1
and
8.2
as follows.

Proof of Proposition
8.1
.

Let
q>0
,
A>1
,
C_{1}>1
be the constants appearing in Proposition
8.3
. Fix
R_{0}>0
with
B(x_{0},R_{0})\subseteq\Omega
, for any
R\in(0,R_{0})
, let

M_{1}=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}u,\quad m_{1}=\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}u,

M_{2}=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)}u,\quad m_{2}=\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},R)}u.

Since
M_{2}-u,u-m_{2}\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
are non-negative bounded in
B(x_{0},R)
, and
-\Delta_{p}^{(L)}(M_{2}-u)=-f\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},R)
,
-\Delta_{p}^{(L)}(u-m_{2})=f\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},R)
, by Proposition
8.3
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)}(M_{2}-u)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}(M_{2}-u)+\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right),

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)}(u-m_{2})^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}(u-m_{2})+\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right),

where

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}(M_{2}-u)

\displaystyle=M_{2}-M_{1},

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}(u-m_{2})

\displaystyle=m_{1}-m_{2}.

By the norm and quasi-norm properties of
L^{q}
-spaces (see
[
24
, Equations (1.1.3) and (1.1.4)]
), there exists
C_{2}\geq 1
depending only on
q
such that

\displaystyle M_{2}-m_{2}\leq C_{2}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)}(M_{2}-u)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)}(u-m_{2})^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}C_{2}\left((M_{2}-M_{1})+(m_{1}-m_{2})+2\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right),

hence

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}u\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{2}-1}{C_{1}C_{2}}\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+2\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

By Lemma
11.2
, for any
\mu\in(0,1)
and any
r\leq R
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},r)}u\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{(1-\mu)\log_{2A}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}}\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+2C_{1}C_{2}\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\mu}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{(1-\mu)\log_{2A}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}}\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+2C_{1}C_{2}C_{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\frac{p\mu}{p-1}}\Psi\left(R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}},

where the second inequality follows from Equation (
2.4
), then there exist
\delta,\mu\in(0,1)
depending only
p,A,C_{1}C_{2}
such that
(1-\mu)\log_{2A}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}\geq\delta
and
\frac{p\mu}{p-1}\geq\delta
, hence

\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},r)}u\leq C_{3}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\delta}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right),

where
C_{3}=\max\{\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1},2C_{1}C_{2}C_{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.2
.

Let
q>0
,
A>1
,
C_{1}>1
be the constants appearing in Proposition
8.4
. Since
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(\Omega)
is non-negative bounded in
\Omega
, for any
R\in(0,r_{0})
, let

M_{1}=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)\cap\Omega}u=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}u,\quad M_{2}=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)\cap\Omega}u=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)}u,

then
0\leq M_{1}\leq M_{2}<+\infty
,
M_{2}-u\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
is non-negative bounded in
B(x_{0},R)
,
M_{2}-\widetilde{u}=M_{2}
q.e. on
B(x_{0},R)\backslash\Omega
,
(M_{2}-u)\wedge M_{2}=M_{2}-u
in
B(x_{0},R)
, and
-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}(M_{2}-u)=-f\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},R)\cap\Omega
, by Proposition
8.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)}\left(M_{2}-u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C_{1}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}(M_{2}-u)+\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right),

where

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}(M_{2}-u)=M_{2}-M_{1}.

Since
X\backslash\Omega
has a
(c,r_{0})
-corkscrew at
x_{0}
and
R\in(0,r_{0})
, we have
B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)\backslash\Omega
contains a ball
B
with radius
\frac{c}{A}R
. Since
M_{2}-\widetilde{u}=M_{2}
q.e. on
B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)\backslash\Omega\supseteq B
, by
VD
, we have

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)}\left(M_{2}-u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\geq\left(\frac{m(B)}{V(x_{0},\frac{1}{A}R)}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}M_{2}\geq\frac{1}{C_{2}}M_{2},

where
C_{2}=C_{VD}^{\frac{2-\log_{2}c}{q}}\geq 1
. Hence

\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2A}R)}u\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{2}-1}{C_{1}C_{2}}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

By Lemma
11.2
, for any
\mu\in(0,1)
and any
R,r\in(0,r_{0})
with
r\leq R
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},r)}u\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{(1-\mu)\log_{2A}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+C_{1}C_{2}\Psi\left(\frac{1}{A}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\mu}R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{(1-\mu)\log_{2A}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)}u+C_{1}C_{2}C_{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\frac{p\mu}{p-1}}\Psi\left(R\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}},

where the second inequality follows from Equation (
2.4
), then there exist
\delta,\mu\in(0,1)
depending only
p,A,C_{1}C_{2}
such that
(1-\mu)\log_{2A}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1}\geq\delta
and
\frac{p\mu}{p-1}\geq\delta
, hence

\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},r)\cap\Omega}u\leq C_{3}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\delta}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},R)\cap\Omega}u+\Psi(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right),

where
C_{3}=\max\{\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{C_{1}C_{2}-1},C_{1}C_{2}C_{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\}
.
∎

We give the proof of “
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
\Rightarrow
\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
” in Theorem
2.1
as follows.

Proof of “
\text{CS}^{(L)}_{\text{weak}}(\Psi)
\Rightarrow
\text{CE}^{(L)}_{\text{strong}}(\Psi)
” in Theorem
2.1
.

We refine the proof of
[
45
, Proposition 3.1]
. For any ball
B=B(x_{0},r)
, by Lemma
3.1
, there exist
c\in(0,1)
, which depends only on
C_{cc}
, and an open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
with
4B\subseteq\Omega\subseteq 5B
such that for any

,

has a
(c,32r)
-corkscrew at

.

By Proposition
3.6
, there exists a unique
u_{\Omega}\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(\Omega)
such that

-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}u_{\Omega}+\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\lvert u_{\Omega}\rvert^{p-2}u_{\Omega}=1\text{ in }\Omega.

By Proposition
3.8
, we have
0\leq u_{\Omega}\leq{\Psi(r)}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
in
X
. Similarly, there exists a unique
v\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(4B)
such that
-\Delta_{p}^{(L)}v+\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\lvert v\rvert^{p-2}v=1
in
4B
. By
[
45
, Proposition 3.18 and Theorem 2.3]
, there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{2B}v\geq\frac{C_{1}}{\left(C_{2}+\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\Psi(4r)\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}\Psi(4r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\geq C_{3}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}},

where
C_{3}>0
depends only on
p,C_{\Psi},C_{1},C_{2}
. Since
4B\subseteq\Omega
, by Proposition
3.8
, we have
u_{\Omega}\geq v
in
4B
. Since
-\Delta^{(L)}_{p}u_{\Omega}=1-\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\lvert u_{\Omega}\rvert^{p-2}u_{\Omega}
in
\Omega
, where
0\leq 1-\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\lvert u_{\Omega}\rvert^{p-2}u_{\Omega}\leq 1
in
\Omega
, an initial application of Propositions
8.1
and
8.2
yields that
u_{\Omega}\in C\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
and
u_{\Omega}=0
on
\partial\Omega
, and hence
u_{\Omega}\in C(X)
with
u_{\Omega}=0
on
