. Hence

\inf_{B}u_{\Omega}\geq\inf_{2B}u_{\Omega}=\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{2B}u_{\Omega}\geq\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{2B}v\geq C_{3}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

(8.10)

Let
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
be the constants appearing in Propositions
8.1
,
8.2
, respectively, and
\delta=\min\{\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\}\in(0,1)
.

We claim that there exists
C_{4}>0
such that

\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert\leq C_{4}\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\text{ for any }x,y\in X.

(8.11)

Indeed, if
x,y\in X\backslash\Omega
, then this result is trivial, hence without loss of generality, we may assume that
x\in\Omega
, then
D=\mathrm{dist}(x,X\backslash\Omega)\in(0,10r)
, and there exists
y_{0}\in\partial\Omega
such that
D\leq d(y_{0},x)<2D
. If
d(x,y)\geq r
, then this result follows directly from the fact that
0\leq u_{\Omega}\leq\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
in
X
, hence we may assume that
d(x,y)<r
.

If
y\in\Omega
and
d(x,y)<D
, then by Proposition
8.1
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{D}\right)^{\delta_{1}}\left(\mathrm{osc}_{B(x,D)}u_{\Omega}+\Psi(D)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{D}\right)^{\delta}\left(\sup_{B(y_{0},3D)\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}+\Psi(D)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

Since
3D<32r
, by Proposition
8.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{B(y_{0},3D)\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta_{2}}\left(\sup_{X}u_{\Omega}+\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

By Equation (
2.4
), we have

\Psi(D)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\leq\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Hence

\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

If
y\in\Omega
and
d(x,y)\geq D
, then
x,y\in B(y_{0},3d(x,y))\subseteq B(y_{0},3r)
, hence by Proposition
8.2
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert\leq 2\sup_{B(y_{0},3d(x,y))\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta_{2}}\left(\sup_{X}u_{\Omega}+\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

If
y\in X\backslash\Omega
, then
D\leq d(x,y)
, hence by Proposition
8.2
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert=u_{\Omega}(x)\leq\sup_{B(y_{0},2D)\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta_{2}}\left(\sup_{X}u_{\Omega}+\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Now we finish the proof of Equation (
8.11
).

We claim that there exists
C_{5}>0
such that

\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\leq C_{5}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s\wedge r)}\text{ for any }x\in X,s>0.

(8.12)

Indeed, by
VD
,

⁢

cap

(
L
)

(
Ψ
)

≤

, there exists a cutoff function
\psi\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}
for
B(x,s)\subseteq B(x,2s)
such that
\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\psi)\lesssim\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\leq\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})=\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}\left(u_{\Omega};u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}^{(L)}\left(u_{\Omega};\psi^{p}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\right)-p\int_{X}\psi^{p-1}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega};\psi).

(8.13)

By Equation (
8.11
), we have

0\leq u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\text{ in }B(x,2s).

(8.14)

Since
-\Delta_{p}^{(L)}u_{\Omega}+\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\lvert u_{\Omega}\rvert^{p-2}u_{\Omega}=1
in
\Omega
, and
\psi^{p}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(\Omega)
is non-negative in
X
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(L)}\left(u_{\Omega};\psi^{p}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}^{(L)}\left(u_{\Omega};\psi^{p}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}\int_{X}\lvert u_{\Omega}\rvert^{p-2}u_{\Omega}\psi^{p}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=\int_{X}\psi^{p}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\mathrm{d}m\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}V(x,s),

(8.15)

where the last inequality follows from Equation (
8.14
) and
VD
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\int_{X}\psi^{p-1}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega};\psi)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{X}\left(u_{\Omega}-\inf_{B(x,2s)}u_{\Omega}\right)^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\psi)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\lesssim$}}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\mathcal{E}^{(L)}(\psi)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}},

where
(\star)
also follows from Equation (
8.14
). Combining this with Equations (
8.13
) and (
8.15
), there exists
C_{6}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})

\displaystyle\leq C_{6}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}V(x,s)+C_{6}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{6}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}V(x,s)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})+C_{7}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)},

where the last inequality follows from Young’s inequality, and

depends only on
p,C_{6}
, hence

\displaystyle\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\leq\int_{X}\psi^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{6}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}V(x,s)+2C_{7}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s)}

\displaystyle\leq 2(C_{6}+C_{7})\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\left(\frac{1}{\Psi(r)}+\frac{1}{\Psi(s)}\right)V(x,s)

\displaystyle\leq 4(C_{6}+C_{7})\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Psi(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s\wedge r)}.

Now we finish the proof of Equation (
8.12
).

Finally, let
\phi=\left(\frac{1}{C_{3}\Psi(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}u_{\Omega}\right)\wedge 1
, then
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(\Omega)\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{(L)}(8B)
. By Equation (
8.10
), we have
\phi=1
in
B
, hence
\phi
is a cutoff function for
B\subseteq 8B
. By Equation (
8.11
), we have

\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert\leq\frac{C_{4}}{C_{3}}\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\text{ for any }x,y\in X,

that is,
\text{CE}^{(L)}(\Psi)\text{-}1
holds. By Equation (
8.12
), for any
x\in X
,
s>0
, we have

\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(\phi)\leq\frac{1}{C_{3}^{p}\Psi(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}}\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(L)}(u_{\Omega})\leq\frac{C_{5}}{C_{3}^{p}}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Psi(s\wedge r)},

that is,
\text{CE}^{(L)}(\Psi)\text{-}2
holds.
∎

9
Two oscillation inequalities

Throughout this section, we always assume that
(\mathcal{E}^{(J)},\mathcal{F}^{(J)})
is a non-local regular
p
-energy associated with
\Upsilon
. The main results of this section are the following interior and boundary oscillation inequalities, which will play an important role in Section
10
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then there exist
\delta\in(0,1)
,
C>0
such that for any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, let
f\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)
satisfy
\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)}\leq 1
, let
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
satisfy
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u=f
in
\Omega
, then for any
x_{0}\in\Omega
and any
R,r>0
with
r\leq R
and
B(x_{0},R)\subseteq\Omega
, we have

\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},r)}u\leq C\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\delta}\left(\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},R);m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

In particular, we have
u\in C(\Omega)
.

Proposition 9.2
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Let
c\in(0,1)
. Then there exist
\delta\in(0,1)
,
C>0
such that for any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, let
f\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)
satisfy
\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega;m)}\leq 1
, let
u\in{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}(\Omega)\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
be non-negative in
\Omega
and satisfy
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u=f
in
\Omega
, let
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
satisfy
X\backslash\Omega
has a
(c,r_{0})
-corkscrew at
x_{0}
for some
r_{0}>0
, then for any
R,r\in(0,r_{0})
with
r\leq R
, we have

\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},r)\cap\Omega}u\leq C\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\delta}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{X}u+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

In particular, we have
\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B(x_{0},r)\cap\Omega}u=0
.

The proofs rely on the following interior and boundary weak Harnack inequalities. In comparison with the local case, namely Propositions
8.3
and
8.4
, special attention must be paid to the tail terms. We refer to
[
33
, Theorems 3.6 and 3.7]
for weak Harnack inequalities for superharmonic functions in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proposition 9.3
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then there exist
q>0
,
A>1
,
C>1
such that for any
x_{0}\in X
and any
R>0
, let
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
be non-negative in
B(x_{0},AR)
, and satisfy
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},AR)
, then we have

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}u^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}u+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left(u_{-};x_{0},AR\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

Proposition 9.4
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then there exist
q>0
,
A>1
,
C>1
such that the following holds. For any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, any
x_{0}\in X
, and any
R>0
, let
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
be non-negative in
B(x_{0},AR)
and satisfy
\widetilde{u}=M
q.e. on
B(x_{0},AR)\backslash\Omega
, for some constant
M\in[0,+\infty)
, and
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},AR)\cap\Omega
, then we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}(u\wedge M)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}(u\wedge M)+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},AR\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

It suffices to prove Proposition
9.4
. Indeed, assuming Proposition
9.4
, by taking
\Omega=B(x_{0},AR)
and
M=\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},AR);m)}<+\infty
, and noting that
u\wedge M=u
in
B(x_{0},AR)
and
(u\wedge M)_{-}=u_{-}
in
X
, we obtain Proposition
9.3
.

Lemma 9.5
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then for any
q>0
, there exists
C>1
such that the following holds. For any bounded open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
, any
x_{0}\in X
, and any
R_{0}>0
, let
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
be non-negative in
B(x_{0},R_{0})
and satisfy
\widetilde{u}=M
q.e. on
B(x_{0},R_{0})\backslash\Omega
for some constant
M\in[0,+\infty)
, and
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},R_{0})\cap\Omega
, then for any
R\in(0,R_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}(u\wedge M)+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{C}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left(u\wedge M+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{-q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{-\frac{1}{q}}.

Proof.

For notational convenience, we may assume that all functions in
\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
are quasi-continuous. If
M=0
, then the result is trivial, hence we may assume that
M>0
. For any

with

and

, for any

let
\overline{u}=u\wedge M+K
, then by Lemma

, we have
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}\overline{u}\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},R_{0})\cap\Omega
. Let

be a cutoff function for

to be chosen later. For any

, let

is non-negative, hence

\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\overline{u};v)\geq-\int_{X}v\mathrm{d}m\geq-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m,

(9.1)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\overline{u};v)=\left(\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}+2\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R+r)}\right)\ldots\mathrm{d}m\mathrm{d}m=I_{1}+2I_{2}.

(9.2)

For

, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}=\int_{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R+r)}\lvert\overline{u}(x)-\overline{u}(y)\rvert^{p-2}(\overline{u}(x)-\overline{u}(y))

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\cdot\phi(x)^{p}\left(\overline{u}(x)^{\alpha}-(M+K)^{\alpha}\right)K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R+r)}\left(\overline{u}(x)-\overline{u}(y)\right)^{p-1}\overline{u}(x)^{\alpha}K^{(J)}(x,y)1_{\overline{u}(x)\geq\overline{u}(y)}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\lesssim$}}\int_{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}(x)^{\alpha+p-1}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\int_{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R+r)}(u(y)\wedge M)_{-}^{p-1}\overline{u}(x)^{\alpha}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I_{21}+I_{22},

where

follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\left(\overline{u}(x)-\overline{u}(y)\right)^{p-1}1_{\overline{u}(x)\geq\overline{u}(y)}=\left({u}(x)\wedge M-{u}(y)\wedge M\right)^{p-1}1_{{u}(x)\wedge M\geq{u}(y)\wedge M}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left(\left(u(x)\wedge M\right)^{p-1}+\left(u(y)\wedge M\right)_{-}^{p-1}\right)\leq 2^{p-1}\left(\overline{u}(x)^{p-1}+\left(u(y)\wedge M\right)_{-}^{p-1}\right),

due to

in

. By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(

)

, we have

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m.

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{\log_{2}(C_{\Upsilon}C_{VD})}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m,

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim I_{21}+I_{22}\lesssim\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{\log_{2}(C_{\Upsilon}C_{VD})}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+\frac{1}{2}r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m.

(9.3)

By Lemma
11.3
, since in

,

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-\frac{2\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert^{p}}{p^{p}\lvert\alpha\rvert}I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},R+r)}(\phi)-\frac{2\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert^{p}}{p^{p}\lvert\alpha\rvert}I_{1},

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},R+r)}\overline{u}^{\alpha+p-1}\mathrm{d}m+\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert^{p}}{\lvert\alpha\rvert}\left(2I_{2}-\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\overline{u};v)\right)

for any

,

,

Proof of Proposition

.

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{1}B}\int_{X\backslash A_{2}B}\left(\overline{u}(x)-\overline{u}(y)\right)^{p-1}\overline{u}(x)^{1-p}K^{(J)}(x,y)1_{\overline{u}(x)\geq\overline{u}(y)}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\lesssim$}}\int_{A_{1}B}\int_{X\backslash A_{2}B}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{A_{1}B}\int_{X\backslash A_{2}B}(u(y)\wedge M)_{-}^{p-1}\overline{u}(x)^{1-p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I_{21}+I_{22}.

where
(\star)
follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\left(\overline{u}(x)-\overline{u}(y)\right)^{p-1}1_{\overline{u}(x)\geq\overline{u}(y)}=\left({u}(x)\wedge M-{u}(y)\wedge M\right)^{p-1}1_{{u}(x)\wedge M\geq{u}(y)\wedge M}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left(\left({u}(x)\wedge M\right)^{p-1}+\left({u}(y)\wedge M\right)_{-}^{p-1}\right)\leq 2^{p-1}\left(\overline{u}(x)^{p-1}+\left({u}(y)\wedge M\right)_{-}^{p-1}\right),

due to
u\geq 0
in
A_{1}B
. By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle I_{21}\leq\int_{A_{1}B}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,(A_{2}-A_{1})r)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)\lesssim\frac{m(A_{1}B)}{\Upsilon(r)}\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

Since
u\geq 0
in
B(x_{0},AR)
, we have

\displaystyle I_{22}=\int_{A_{1}B}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},AR)}(u(y)\wedge M)_{-}^{p-1}\overline{u}(x)^{1-p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y).

For any
x\in A_{1}B\subseteq B(x_{0},36A_{2}R)
and
y\in X\backslash B(x_{0},AR)=X\backslash B(x_{0},48A_{2}R)
, by the triangle inequality, we have
\frac{4}{7}d(x,y)\leq d(x_{0},y)\leq 4d(x,y)
, then by

⁢

K

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

,
VD
and Equation (
2.1
), we have

K^{(J)}(x,y)\asymp K^{(J)}(x_{0},y),

hence

\displaystyle I_{22}\lesssim\left(\int_{A_{1}B}\overline{u}^{1-p}\mathrm{d}m\right)\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},AR)}\left(u(y)\wedge M\right)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$=$}}\left(\int_{A_{1}B}\overline{u}^{1-p}\mathrm{d}m\right)\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},R)}\left(u(y)\wedge M\right)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}\mathrm{Tail}((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R)^{p-1}\int_{A_{1}B}\overline{u}^{1-p}\mathrm{d}m\overset{(\ast)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}\frac{m(A_{1}B)}{\Upsilon(R)}\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(r)},

where
(\dagger)
follows from the fact that
u\geq 0
in
B(x_{0},AR)
, and
(\ast)
follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)\leq K\leq\overline{u}\text{ in }A_{1}B.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim I_{21}+I_{22}\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

Combining this with Equations (
9.7
)–(
9.10
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B}\int_{B}\lvert\log{\overline{u}(x)}-\log{\overline{u}(y)}\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(r)}-I_{1}=\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(r)}+2I_{2}-\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\overline{u};v)\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

By Equation (
9.6
), we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B}\lvert\log\overline{u}-(\log\overline{u})_{B}\rvert\mathrm{d}m\leq C_{1},

where
C_{1}>0
is some constant. By Lemma
3.3
, there exist
C_{4},C_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\exp\left(\frac{C_{5}}{2C_{1}}\log\overline{u}\right)\mathrm{d}m\right)\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{5}}{2C_{1}}\log\overline{u}\right)\mathrm{d}m\right)\leq\left(C_{4}+1\right)^{2}.

Let
q=\frac{C_{5}}{2C_{1}}
, then

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left({u}\wedge M+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left({u}\wedge M+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{-q}\mathrm{d}m\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{4}+1\right)^{2}.

(9.11)

Since
u\geq 0
in
B(x_{0},AR)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)^{p-1}=\frac{1}{\Upsilon(AR)}\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},AR\right)^{p-1}.

Combining this with Equation (
9.11
) and Lemma
9.5
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}(u\wedge M)+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},AR\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}R)}(u\wedge M)+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left(u\wedge M+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left((u\wedge M)_{-};x_{0},R\right)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},R)}\left(u\wedge M\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

∎

Using the two weak Harnack inequalities established above, we prove the oscillation inequalities. In comparison with the local case, namely Propositions
8.1
and
8.2
, special care must be taken with the tail terms. Our proof follows the induction argument of
[
20
, Lemma 5.1]
, in particular the reasoning leading to Equation (5.6) therein.

We need the following result for preparation; see also
[
19
, Lemma 4.2]
,
[
16
, Lemma 2.6]
.

Lemma 9.6
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any ball
B=B(x_{0},R)
, let
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
satisfy
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u\geq-1
in
B
, then we have

\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)\leq C\left(\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B;m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

Proof.

Firstly, we assume that
k=\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B;m)}>0
. Let
w=u-2k
, then
-3k\leq w\leq 0
in
B
. By
VD
,

⁢

cap

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

≤

, there exists a cutoff function
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}
for
\frac{1}{2}B\subseteq\frac{3}{4}B
such that
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\phi)\lesssim\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}
, then
\phi^{p}w\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\frac{3}{4}B)\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(B)
is
non-positive
. By assumption, we have

\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(w;\phi^{p}w)=\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u;\phi^{p}w)\leq-\int_{X}\phi^{p}w\mathrm{d}m\leq 3km(B),

(9.12)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(w;\phi^{p}w)

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{B}\int_{B}+2\int_{B}\int_{X\backslash B}\right)\lvert w(x)-w(y)\rvert^{p-2}(w(x)-w(y))\left(\phi(x)^{p}w(x)-\phi(y)^{p}w(y)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+2I_{2}.

(9.13)

For
I_{1}
, for
m
-a.e.
x,y\in B
, we have
\lvert w(x)\rvert,\lvert w(y)\rvert\leq 3k
, then by Lemma
11.6
, there exists
C>0
depending only on
p
, such that

\displaystyle\lvert w(x)-w(y)\rvert^{p-2}(w(x)-w(y))\left(\phi(x)^{p}w(x)-\phi(y)^{p}w(y)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\lvert w(x)-w(y)\rvert^{p}\left(\phi(x)^{p}+\phi(y)^{p}\right)-{C}\left(\lvert w(x)\rvert^{p}+\lvert w(y)\rvert^{p}\right)\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\geq-{C}\cdot 2\cdot(3k)^{p}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}=-2\cdot 3^{p}Ck^{p}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p},

hence

\displaystyle I_{1}\gtrsim-k^{p}\int_{B}\int_{B}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\geq-k^{p}\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\phi)\gtrsim-k^{p}\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}.

(9.14)

For
I_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}=\int_{\frac{3}{4}B}\int_{X\backslash B}\lvert w(x)-w(y)\rvert^{p-2}(w(x)-w(y))\phi(x)^{p}w(x)K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y).

For
m
-a.e.
x\in\frac{3}{4}B
, we have
-k\leq u(x)\leq k
, hence

\displaystyle\lvert w(x)-w(y)\rvert^{p-2}(w(x)-w(y))w(x)=\lvert u(y)-u(x)\rvert^{p-2}(u(y)-u(x))(2k-u(x))

\displaystyle=\lvert u(y)-u(x)\rvert^{p-2}(u(y)-u(x))_{+}(2k-u(x))-\lvert u(y)-u(x)\rvert^{p-2}(u(y)-u(x))_{-}(2k-u(x))

\displaystyle\geq k(u(y)-u(x))_{+}^{p-1}-3k(u(y)-u(x))_{-}^{p-1}\geq k(u(y)-k)_{+}^{p-1}-3k(u(y)-k)_{-}^{p-1},

which gives

\displaystyle I_{2}\geq k\int_{\frac{3}{4}B}\int_{X\backslash B}(u(y)-k)_{+}^{p-1}\phi(x)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-3k\int_{\frac{3}{4}B}\int_{X\backslash B}(u(y)-k)_{-}^{p-1}\phi(x)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I_{21}-3I_{22}.

(9.15)

For
I_{21}
, since
u(y)_{+}^{p-1}\leq 2^{p-1}\left((u(y)-k)_{+}^{p-1}+k^{p-1}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle I_{21}\geq k\int_{\frac{1}{2}B}\int_{X\backslash B}(u(y)-k)_{+}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{k}{2^{p-1}}\int_{\frac{1}{2}B}\int_{X\backslash B}u(y)_{+}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)-k^{p}\int_{\frac{1}{2}B}\int_{X\backslash B}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I_{211}-I_{212}.

(9.16)

For
I_{22}
, since
(u(y)-k)_{-}^{p-1}\leq 2^{p-1}\left(u(y)_{-}^{p-1}+k^{p-1}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle I_{22}\lesssim k\int_{\frac{3}{4}B}\int_{X\backslash B}u(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)+k^{p}\int_{\frac{3}{4}B}\int_{X\backslash B}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y).

For any
x\in\frac{3}{4}B
and
y\in X\backslash B
, by the triangle inequality, we have
\frac{4}{7}d(x,y)\leq d(x_{0},y)\leq 4d(x,y)
, then by

⁢

K

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

,
VD
and Equation (
2.1
), we have

K^{(J)}(x,y)\asymp K^{(J)}(x_{0},y).

Hence

\displaystyle I_{211}

\displaystyle\asymp k\int_{\frac{1}{2}B}\int_{X\backslash B}u(y)_{+}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\asymp k\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)^{p-1},

(9.17)

\displaystyle I_{212}

\displaystyle\asymp k^{p}\int_{\frac{1}{2}B}\int_{X\backslash B}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\asymp k^{p}\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)},

(9.18)

and

\displaystyle I_{22}\lesssim k\int_{\frac{3}{4}B}\int_{X\backslash B}u(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)+k^{p}\int_{\frac{3}{4}B}\int_{X\backslash B}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\lesssim k\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)^{p-1}+k^{p}\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}.

(9.19)

Combining Equations (
9.12
)–(
9.19
), we have

\displaystyle k\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)^{p-1}\lesssim k\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)^{p-1}+k^{p}\frac{m(B)}{\Upsilon(R)}+km(B),

that is,

\displaystyle k\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)^{p-1}\lesssim k^{p}+k\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)^{p-1}+k\Upsilon(R).

Since
k=\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B;m)}>0
, we have

\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B;m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Secondly, we assume that
\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B;m)}=0
. For any
\varepsilon>0
, we have
u+\varepsilon\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
satisfies
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}(u+\varepsilon)\geq-1
in
B
, and
u+\varepsilon=\varepsilon
in
B
. By above, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}((u+\varepsilon)_{+};x_{0},R)\lesssim\varepsilon+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}((u+\varepsilon)_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, by Fatou’s lemma, we have

\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)\lesssim\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

∎

We give the proofs of Propositions
9.1
and
9.2
as follows.

Proof of Proposition
9.1
.

Let
q>0
,
A_{1}>1
be the constants appearing in Proposition
9.3
. Let
a>1
be chosen sufficiently large (to be specified later) such that
A=2aA_{1}
satisfies
A^{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}\geq 2^{p}
, where
\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}
is a constant appearing in Equation (
2.1
). For any
k\geq 0
, let
R_{k}=\frac{1}{A^{k}}R
and
B_{k}=B(x_{0},R_{k})
. Let
\alpha\in[\frac{1}{2},1)
be chosen later and

K=2\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},R);m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

The proof proceeds by choosing suitable
a
and
\alpha
, and applying induction to show that, for any
k\geq 0
, we have

\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B_{k}}u\leq\alpha^{k}K=\alpha^{k}\left(2\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{0};m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

(9.20)

To begin with, using the a priori bounds on
a
and
\alpha
, we have

\alpha^{p-1}A^{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}\geq\frac{1}{2^{p-1}}2^{p}=2.

(9.21)

We emphasize that, in the following proof, the implicit constants in
\lesssim
and
\asymp
are
independent
of
\alpha
and
A
(or, equivalently,
a
). For
k=0
, the result is obvious. Assume that the result holds for
j=0,\ldots,k
. For
k+1
, let
v=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-u
, then
v\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
is non-negative in
B_{k}
and satisfies
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}v=-f\geq-1
in
B_{k}
. Since
B_{k}=2aA_{1}B_{k+1}\supseteq 2A_{1}B_{k+1}
, by Proposition
9.3
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{2B_{k+1}}v^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k+1}}v+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left(v_{-};x_{0},2A_{1}R_{k+1}\right)+\Upsilon(2R_{k+1})^{\frac{1}{p-1}},

where

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k+1}}v=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k+1}}u.

By Equations (
2.1
) and (
9.21
), we have

\displaystyle\Upsilon(2R_{k+1})\lesssim\left(\frac{2R_{k+1}}{R}\right)^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}\Upsilon(R)\asymp\frac{1}{A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}(k+1)}}\Upsilon(R)=\frac{1}{\left(\alpha^{p-1}A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}\right)^{k+1}}\alpha^{(p-1)(k+1)}\Upsilon(R)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha^{p-1}A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}}\alpha^{(p-1)(k+1)}\Upsilon(R)\leq\frac{1}{A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}}\left(\alpha^{k}K\right)^{p-1}.

(9.22)

Since
v\geq 0
in
B_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left(v_{-};x_{0},2A_{1}R_{k+1}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\Upsilon(2A_{1}R_{k+1})\int_{X\backslash 2A_{1}B_{k+1}}v(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\Upsilon(2A_{1}R_{k+1})\int_{X\backslash B_{k}}v(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\Upsilon(2A_{1}R_{k+1})\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\int_{B_{j}\backslash B_{j+1}}+\int_{X\backslash B_{0}}\right)v(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y).

By the induction assumption, for any
j=0,\ldots,k-1
, we have

v_{-}=\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-u\right)_{-}\leq\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B_{j}}u\leq\alpha^{j}K\text{ in }B_{j}.

By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\int_{B_{j}\backslash B_{j+1}}v(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R_{j+1})}\left(\alpha^{j}K\right)^{p-1}.

Since
v_{-}=(u-\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u)_{+}\leq u_{+}+\lvert\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u\rvert\leq u_{+}+K
in
X
, by

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle\int_{X\backslash B_{0}}v(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)^{p-1}+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}K^{p-1}.

Since
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u=f\geq-1
in
B(x_{0},R)
, by Lemma
9.6
, we have

\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{+};x_{0},R)\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},R);m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\leq K,

which gives

\int_{X\backslash B_{0}}v(y)_{-}^{p-1}K^{(J)}(x_{0},y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\lesssim\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}K^{p-1}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}\left(v_{-};x_{0},2A_{1}R_{k+1}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Upsilon(2A_{1}R_{k+1})\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R_{j+1})}(\alpha^{j}K)^{p-1}+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(R)}K^{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim K^{p-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\alpha^{(p-1)j}\frac{\Upsilon(2A_{1}R_{k+1})}{\Upsilon(R_{j})}\overset{(\diamond)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\lesssim$}}K^{p-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\alpha^{(p-1)j}\left(\frac{2A_{1}R_{k+1}}{R_{j}}\right)^{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\asymp K^{p-1}\frac{1}{A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}(k+1)}}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left(\alpha^{p-1}A^{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}\right)^{j}\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}K^{p-1}\frac{1}{A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}(k+1)}}\frac{\left(\alpha^{p-1}A^{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}\right)^{k+1}}{\alpha^{p-1}A^{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}-1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}K^{p-1}\frac{\alpha^{(p-1)(k+1)}}{\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{p-1}A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}}=\frac{2}{A^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}}\left(\alpha^{k}K\right)^{p-1},

(9.23)

where
(\diamond)
follows from Equation (
2.1
), and
(\star)
and
(\dagger)
follow from Equation (
9.21
). Hence

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{2B_{k+1}}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k+1}}u+\frac{1}{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}\alpha^{k}\left(\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},R);m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

Similarly, by considering
u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
, which is non-negative in
B_{k}
and satisfies
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}(u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u)=f\geq-1
in
B_{k}
, noting that
\left(u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u\right)_{-}\leq u_{-}+\lvert\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u\rvert\leq u_{-}+K
in
X
, we also have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{2B_{k+1}}\left(u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k+1}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u+\frac{1}{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}\alpha^{k}\left(\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},R);m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

Hence there exists
C_{1}>1
such that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{2B_{k+1}}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k+1}}u+\frac{1}{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}\alpha^{k}K\right),

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{2B_{k+1}}\left(u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k+1}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u+\frac{1}{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}\alpha^{k}K\right).

By the norm and quasi-norm properties of
L^{q}
-spaces (see
[
24
, Equations (1.1.3) and (1.1.4)]
), there exists
C_{2}\geq 1
depending only on
q
such that

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{2B_{k+1}}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k}}u-u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{2B_{k+1}}\left(u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}C_{2}\left(\left(\sup_{B_{k}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k}}u\right)-\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B_{k+1}}u-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{k+1}}u\right)+\frac{2}{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}\alpha^{k}K\right),

hence

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B_{k+1}}u\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{2}-1}{C_{1}C_{2}}\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B_{k}}u+\frac{2}{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}\alpha^{k}K\leq\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{2}-1}{C_{1}C_{2}}+\frac{2}{{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}}\right)\alpha^{k}K,

where the second inequality follows from the induction assumption. First, recall that
A=2aA_{1}
, choose
a>1
, depending only on
p,\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)},C_{1},C_{2}
, sufficiently large such that
\frac{C_{1}C_{2}-1}{C_{1}C_{2}}+\frac{2}{{A^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}}}<1
. Second, choose

. Then,
\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B_{k+1}}u\leq\alpha^{k+1}K
, that is, Equation (
9.20
) holds for
k+1
. By induction, Equation (
9.20
) holds for any

.

For any

, there exists a unique integer
k\geq 0
such that
R_{k+1}<r\leq R_{k}
, then by Equation (

), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B(x_{0},r)}u\leq\operatorname*{ess\,osc}_{B_{k}}u\leq\alpha^{k}K

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\alpha}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\delta}\left(\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x_{0},R);m)}+\mathrm{Tail}^{(J)}(u_{-};x_{0},R)+\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right),

where
\delta=-\log_{A}{\alpha}>0
.
∎

Proof of Proposition

.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq X
be a bounded open subset, and let
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
and
\phi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
be non-negative in
X
. For any
\lambda>0
, let
u_{\lambda}=u+\lambda
, then we have
\frac{\phi^{p}}{u_{\lambda}^{p-1}}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)
and

\mathcal{E}^{(J)}\left(u;\frac{\phi^{p}}{u_{\lambda}^{p-1}}\right)\leq C\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\phi),

where

is some constant depending only on
p
.

Proof.

By the Markovian property (see also
[
15
, Proposition A.3]
), we have
\frac{\phi^{p}}{u_{\lambda}^{p-1}}\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}\left(u;\frac{\phi^{p}}{u_{\lambda}^{p-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}+2\int_{\Omega}\int_{X\backslash\Omega}\right)\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p-2}\left(u(x)-u(y)\right)\left(\frac{\phi(x)^{p}}{u_{\lambda}(x)^{p-1}}-\frac{\phi(y)^{p}}{u_{\lambda}(y)^{p-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\cdot K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+2I_{2}.

For
I_{1}
, since both
u
and
\phi
are non-negative in
X
, by Lemma
11.5
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq-C_{1}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\log u_{\lambda}(x)-\log u_{\lambda}(y)\rvert^{p}\min\{\phi(x)^{p},\phi(y)^{p}\}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt+C_{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

where
C_{1},C_{2}>0
depend only on
p
. For
I_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}=\int_{\Omega}\int_{X\backslash\Omega}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p-2}\left(u(x)-u(y)\right)\frac{\phi(x)^{p}}{u_{\lambda}(x)^{p-1}}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}\int_{X\backslash\Omega}\left(u(x)-u(y)\right)^{p-1}\frac{\phi(x)^{p}}{u_{\lambda}(x)^{p-1}}K^{(J)}(x,y)1_{u(x)\geq u(y)}m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\overset{(\diamond)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}\int_{\Omega}\int_{X\backslash\Omega}u_{\lambda}(x)^{p-1}\frac{\phi(x)^{p}}{u_{\lambda}(x)^{p-1}}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\int_{X\backslash\Omega}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y),

where
(\diamond)
follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\left(u(x)-u(y)\right)^{p-1}1_{u(x)\geq u(y)}\leq u(x)^{p-1}1_{u(x)\geq u(y)}\leq u_{\lambda}(x)^{p-1},

due to
u\geq 0
in
X
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}\left(u;\frac{\phi^{p}}{u_{\lambda}^{p-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}+2\int_{\Omega}\int_{X\backslash\Omega}\right)\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)\leq C\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\phi),

where
C=\max\{C_{2},1\}
.
∎

The following result in the non-local setting parallels
[
45
, Proposition 3.18]
in the local setting. The proof follows the same line of argument as therein, by applying Propositions
3.9
and
10.1
, and is therefore omitted.

Proposition 10.3
.

Assume
VD
,
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
. Then there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for any
\lambda>0
and any ball
B=B(x_{0},R)
, for any
u\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(B)
satisfying that
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u+\lambda\lvert u\rvert^{p-2}u=1
in
B
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{B}u

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{p-1}}},

\displaystyle\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{\frac{1}{4}B}u

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{1}}{\left(C_{2}+\lambda\Upsilon(R)\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}\Upsilon(R)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

We give the proof of “
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
\Rightarrow
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
” in Theorem
2.2
as follows.

Proof of “
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
\Rightarrow
\text{CE}^{(J)}_{\text{strong}}(\Upsilon)
” in Theorem
2.2
.

For any ball
B=B(x_{0},r)
, by Lemma
3.1
, there exist
c\in(0,1)
, which depends only on
C_{cc}
, and an open subset
\Omega\subseteq X
with

such that for any

,

has a

-corkscrew at

.

By Proposition

, there exists a unique

such that

-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u_{\Omega}+\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\lvert u_{\Omega}\rvert^{p-2}u_{\Omega}=1\text{ in }\Omega.

By Proposition

in
X
. Similarly, there exists a unique

such that

in

. By Proposition

, there exist

such that

where
C_{3}>0
depends only on
p,C_{\Upsilon},C_{1},C_{2}
. Since

, by Proposition

, we have
u_{\Omega}\geq v
in

. Since
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u_{\Omega}=1-\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}\lvert u_{\Omega}\rvert^{p-2}u_{\Omega}
in
\Omega
, where

in
\Omega

and

yields that

and

with

on

(10.1)

Let
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
be the constants appearing in Propositions

and

, respectively, and
\delta=\min\{\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1},\frac{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}}{2p}\}\in(0,1)
.

We claim that there exists
C_{4}>0
such that

\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert\leq C_{4}\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\text{ for any }x,y\in X.

(10.2)

Indeed, if
x,y\in X\backslash\Omega
, then this result is trivial, hence without loss of generality, we may assume that
x\in\Omega
, then

in

If

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{D}\right)^{\delta}\left(\sup_{B(y_{0},3D)\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}+\Upsilon(D)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right).

Since

\displaystyle\sup_{B(y_{0},3D)\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta_{2}}\left(\sup_{X}u_{\Omega}+\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

), we have

\Upsilon(D)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\frac{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{p-1}}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Hence

\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

If

and

, then

, hence by Proposition

, we have

\displaystyle\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert\leq 2\sup_{B(y_{0},3d(x,y))\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta_{2}}\left(\sup_{X}u_{\Omega}+\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

If
y\in X\backslash\Omega
, then

, hence by Proposition
9.2
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert u_{\Omega}(x)-u_{\Omega}(y)\rvert=u_{\Omega}(x)\leq\sup_{B(y_{0},2D)\cap\Omega}u_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{D}{r}\right)^{\delta_{2}}\left(\sup_{X}u_{\Omega}+\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{r}\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Now we finish the proof of Equation (
10.2
).

We claim that there exists
C_{5}>0
such that

\int_{B(x,s)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(u_{\Omega})\leq C_{5}\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s\wedge r)}\text{ for any }x\in X,s>0.

(10.3)

Indeed, by

,

)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(\psi)\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}\leq\left(\frac{s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{\delta}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}.

) and

. On the other hand, we have

⁢

)

and

\displaystyle\lesssim\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\frac{V(x,s)}{\Upsilon(s)}\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{2^{n}s}{r}\wedge 1\right)^{p\delta}\frac{1}{2^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}n}},

where the last inequality follows from

). Recall the following elementary result: for any

with

, and any

. Since

).

Finally, let
\phi=\left(\frac{1}{C_{3}\Upsilon(r)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}u_{\Omega}\right)\wedge 1
, then

holds. By Equation (
10.3
), for any
x\in X
,
s>0
, we have

that is,
\text{CE}^{(J)}(\Upsilon)\text{-}2
holds.
∎

11
Some elementary results

In this section, we give some elementary results used in the previous sections.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
A,B\geq 1
,
\kappa>1
be constants. Let
\{I_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
be a sequence of non-negative real-numbers satisfying that

I_{k+1}\leq A^{\frac{1}{\kappa^{k}}}B^{\frac{k}{\kappa^{k}}}I_{k}\text{ for any }k\geq 0.

Then

I_{k}\leq A^{\frac{\kappa}{\kappa-1}}B^{\frac{\kappa}{(\kappa-1)^{2}}}I_{0}\text{ for any }k\geq 0.

The proof is a routine iteration argument and is therefore omitted.

Lemma 11.2

(
[
22
, Lemma 8.23]
)
.

Let
R_{0}\in(0,+\infty]
and
\varphi,\psi:(0,R_{0})\to[0,+\infty)
two increasing functions. Assume that there exist
\tau,\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

\varphi(\tau R)\leq\gamma\varphi(R)+\psi(R)\text{ for any }R\in(0,R_{0}).

Then for any
\mu\in(0,1)
and any
R,r\in(0,R_{0})
with
r\leq R
, we have

\varphi(r)\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{(1-\mu)\log_{\tau}\gamma}\varphi(R)+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\psi\left(\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\mu}R\right).

The following result is a variant of
[
33
, Lemma A.1]
.

Lemma 11.3
.

Let
K>0
,
M\geq 0
,
\alpha<-(p-1)
be constants and let
F,G:[0,M]\to\mathbb{R}
be given by

\displaystyle F(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left((x+K)^{\alpha}-(M+K)^{\alpha}\right),

\displaystyle G(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{\alpha+p-1}\left(x+K\right)^{\frac{\alpha+p-1}{p}}.

Then there exists
C>0
depending only on
p
such that for any
x,y\in[0,M]
and any
a,b\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert x-y\rvert^{p-2}(x-y)\left(F(x)a^{p}-F(y)b^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}-C\left(1+\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{\lvert\alpha\rvert}\right)^{p}\right)\max\{\lvert G(x)\rvert^{p},\lvert G(y)\rvert^{p}\}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}.

We need the following result.

Lemma 11.4

(
[
20
, Lemma 3.1]
)
.

Let
p\in[1,+\infty)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Then for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert a\rvert^{p}\leq\lvert b\rvert^{p}+c_{p}\varepsilon\lvert b\rvert^{p}+\left(1+c_{p}\varepsilon\right)\varepsilon^{1-p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p},

where
c_{p}=(p-1)\Gamma\left(\max\{1,p-2\}\right)
and
\Gamma
stands for the standard Gamma function, hence

\max\{\lvert a\rvert^{p},\lvert b\rvert^{p}\}\leq\left(1+c_{p}\varepsilon\right)\min\{\lvert a\rvert^{p},\lvert b\rvert^{p}\}+\left(1+c_{p}\varepsilon\right)\varepsilon^{1-p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}.

(11.1)

Proof of Lemma
11.3
.

It is easy to see that both
F^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime}
are positive and monotone decreasing, and satisfy that for any
x\in[0,M]
, we have
G^{\prime}(x)^{p}=F^{\prime}(x)
and

\frac{\lvert F(x)\rvert}{G^{\prime}(x)^{p-1}}\leq\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{p\lvert\alpha\rvert}\lvert G(x)\rvert.

(11.2)

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
x>y
, then

\displaystyle\text{LHS}=(x-y)^{p-1}\left(F(x)a^{p}-F(y)b^{p}\right)

\displaystyle=(x-y)^{p-1}\left(F(x)-F(y)\right)a^{p}+(x-y)^{p-1}F(y)\left(a^{p}-b^{p}\right).

By Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle F(x)-F(y)=\int_{y}^{x}F^{\prime}(z)\mathrm{d}z=\int_{y}^{x}G^{\prime}(z)^{p}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(x-y)^{p-1}}\left(\int_{y}^{x}G^{\prime}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)^{p}=\frac{1}{(x-y)^{p-1}}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}.

(11.3)

Since
G^{\prime}
is positive and monotone decreasing, we have

\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert=\int_{y}^{x}G^{\prime}(z)\mathrm{d}z\geq(x-y)G^{\prime}(y).

(11.4)

Moreover, we have

\lvert a^{p}-b^{p}\rvert=\lvert p\int_{a\wedge b}^{a\vee b}z^{p-1}\mathrm{d}z\rvert\leq p\max\{a^{p-1},b^{p-1}\}\lvert a-b\rvert.

(11.5)

Hence for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\text{LHS}\overset{(\diamond)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\geq$}}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}a^{p}-(x-y)^{p-1}\lvert F(y)\rvert\cdot\lvert a^{p}-b^{p}\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\geq$}}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\min\{a^{p},b^{p}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt-(x-y)^{p-1}\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{p\lvert\alpha\rvert}G^{\prime}(y)^{p-1}\lvert G(y)\rvert\right)\left(p\max\{a^{p-1},b^{p-1}\}\lvert a-b\rvert\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\circledast)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\geq$}}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{1}{1+c_{p}\varepsilon}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}-\varepsilon^{1-p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt-\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{\lvert\alpha\rvert}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p-1}\lvert G(y)\rvert\max\{a^{p-1},b^{p-1}\}\lvert a-b\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(\dagger)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\geq$}}\frac{1}{1+c_{p}\varepsilon}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}-\varepsilon^{1-p}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt-\frac{1}{4}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}-C_{1}\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{\lvert\alpha\rvert}\right)^{p}\lvert G(y)\rvert^{p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{1}{1+c_{p}\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt-\left(2^{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}+C_{1}\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{\lvert\alpha\rvert}\right)^{p}\right)\max\{\lvert G(x)\rvert^{p},\lvert G(y)\rvert^{p}\}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p},

where
(\diamond)
follows from Equation (
11.3
),
(\star)
follows from Equations (
11.2
) and (
11.5
),
(\circledast)
follows from Equations (
11.1
) and (
11.4
),
(\dagger)
follows from Young’s inequality, and
C_{1}>0
depends only on
p
. Taking
\varepsilon=\min\{1,\frac{1}{3c_{p}}\}\in(0,1]
, which depends only on
p
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert x-y\rvert^{p-2}(x-y)\left(F(x)a^{p}-F(y)b^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\lvert G(x)-G(y)\rvert^{p}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}-C\left(1+\left(\frac{\lvert\alpha+p-1\rvert}{\lvert\alpha\rvert}\right)^{p}\right)\max\{\lvert G(x)\rvert^{p},\lvert G(y)\rvert^{p}\}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p},

where
C=\max\{2^{p}\varepsilon^{1-p},C_{1}\}
.
∎

Lemma 11.5

(
[
33
, Lemma A.4]
)
.

There exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
depending only on
p
such that for any
K>0
and any
M\geq 0
, for any
x,y\in[0,M]
and any
a,b\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert x-y\rvert^{p-2}(x-y)\left(\left((x+K)^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)a^{p}-\left((y+K)^{1-p}-(M+K)^{1-p}\right)b^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-C_{1}\lvert\log(x+K)-\log(y+K)\rvert^{p}\min\{a^{p},b^{p}\}+C_{2}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}.

Moreover, letting
M\to+\infty
, we obtain that for any
K>0
and any
x,y,a,b\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert x-y\rvert^{p-2}(x-y)\left((x+K)^{1-p}a^{p}-(y+K)^{1-p}b^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-C_{1}\lvert\log(x+K)-\log(y+K)\rvert^{p}\min\{a^{p},b^{p}\}+C_{2}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}.

Lemma 11.6
.

There exists
C>0
depending only on
p
such that for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
and any
a,b\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert x-y\rvert^{p-2}(x-y)(a^{p}x-b^{p}y)\geq\frac{1}{4}\lvert x-y\rvert^{p}(a^{p}+b^{p})-C\left(\lvert x\rvert^{p}+\lvert y\rvert^{p}\right)\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
x>y
, then

\displaystyle\text{LHS}=(x-y)^{p-1}\left(a^{p}x-b^{p}x+b^{p}x-b^{p}y\right)=(x-y)^{p}b^{p}+(x-y)^{p-1}x(a^{p}-b^{p}).

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, by Equation (
11.1
), we have

\displaystyle b^{p}\geq\min\{a^{p},b^{p}\}\geq\frac{1}{1+c_{p}\varepsilon}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}-\varepsilon^{1-p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2(1+c_{p}\varepsilon)}\left(a^{p}+b^{p}\right)-\varepsilon^{1-p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}.

Together with Equation (
11.5
), we have

\displaystyle\text{LHS}\geq\frac{1}{2(1+c_{p}\varepsilon)}(x-y)^{p}\left(a^{p}+b^{p}\right)-\varepsilon^{1-p}(x-y)^{p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-(x-y)^{p-1}\lvert x\rvert\left(p\max\{a^{p-1},b^{p-1}\}\lvert a-b\rvert\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\geq$}}\frac{1}{2(1+c_{p}\varepsilon)}(x-y)^{p}\left(a^{p}+b^{p}\right)-2^{p-1}\varepsilon^{1-p}\left(\lvert x\rvert^{p}+\lvert y\rvert^{p}\right)\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-\frac{1}{8}(x-y)^{p}\max\{a^{p},b^{p}\}-C_{1}\lvert x\rvert^{p}\lvert a-b\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{1}{2(1+c_{p}\varepsilon)}-\frac{1}{8}\right)(x-y)^{p}\left(a^{p}+b^{p}\right)-\left(2^{p-1}\varepsilon^{1-p}+C_{1}\right)\left(\lvert x\rvert^{p}+\lvert y\rvert^{p}\right)\lvert a-b\rvert^{p},

where
(\star)
follows from Young’s inequality and
C_{1}>0
depends only on
p
. Taking
\varepsilon=\min\{1,\frac{1}{3c_{p}}\}\in(0,1]
, which depends only on
p
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert x-y\rvert^{p-2}(x-y)(a^{p}x-b^{p}y)\geq\frac{1}{4}\lvert x-y\rvert^{p}(a^{p}+b^{p})-C\left(\lvert x\rvert^{p}+\lvert y\rvert^{p}\right)\lvert a-b\rvert^{p},

where
C=2^{p-1}\varepsilon^{1-p}+C_{1}
.
∎

Lemma 11.7
.

Assume
VD
,

⁢

K

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

. Then

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

,

⁢

PI

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

hold.

Proof.

First,

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

follows directly by noting that for any
x\in X
and any
r>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{X\backslash B(x,r)}\frac{1}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\int_{B(x,2^{n+1}r)\backslash B(x,2^{n}r)}\frac{1}{V(x,d(x,y))\Upsilon(d(x,y))}m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{V(x,2^{n+1}r)}{V(x,2^{n}r)\Upsilon(2^{n}r)}\leq\left(C_{\Upsilon}C_{VD}\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{2^{\beta_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}n}}\right)\frac{1}{\Upsilon(r)}.

Second, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(x,r)}\lvert f-f_{B(x,r)}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\frac{1}{V(x,r)}\int_{B(x,r)}\int_{B(x,r)}\lvert f(y)-f(z)\rvert^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x,r)}\int_{B(x,r)}\frac{\lvert f(y)-f(z)\rvert^{p}}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}\frac{V(y,d(y,z))}{V(x,r)}\Upsilon(d(y,z))m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z),

where

\Upsilon(d(y,z))\leq\Upsilon(2r)\leq C_{\Upsilon}\Upsilon(r),

and by
VD
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{V(y,d(y,z))}{V(x,r)}\leq C_{VD}\left(\frac{d(x,y)+2r}{r}\right)^{\log_{2}C_{VD}}\leq C_{VD}^{3}.

Hence

\int_{B(x,r)}\lvert f-f_{B(x,r)}\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq C_{\Upsilon}C_{VD}^{3}\Upsilon(r)\int_{B(x,r)}\int_{B(x,r)}\frac{\lvert f(y)-f(z)\rvert^{p}}{V(y,d(y,z))\Upsilon(d(y,z))}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}z),

then

⁢

PI

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

follows from

⁢

K

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

.
∎

Lemma 11.8
.

Assume

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

. Then

⁢

CS

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

-
2

is equivalent to

\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(\phi)\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}(f)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

up to suitable modifications of the constants
C_{1},C_{2}
.

Proof.

“
\Leftarrow
”: Obvious. “
\Rightarrow
”: It follows easily by noting that

\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(\phi)=\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}(\phi)+I,

where

\displaystyle I=\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{0},A_{1}r)}\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\phi(x)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x_{0},A_{1}r)}\lvert f(x)\rvert^{p}\left(\int_{X\backslash B(x,(A_{2}-A_{1})r)}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)\right)m(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{T}}{\Upsilon((A_{2}-A_{1})r)}\int_{B(x,A_{1}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq\frac{C}{\Upsilon(r)}\int_{B(x,A_{2}r)}\lvert f\rvert^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

where
C>0
depends only on
A_{1},A_{2},C_{\Upsilon},C_{T}
.
∎

Lemma 11.9
.

Assume
VD
,

⁢

T

(

)

(
Υ
)

. Then
\text{CS}^{(J)}_{\text{weak}}(\Upsilon)
implies

⁢

cap

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

≤

.

Proof.

By taking
f\equiv 1\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
in Lemma
11.8
, we have

\int_{B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(\phi)\leq C_{2}\frac{V(x_{0},A_{2}r)}{\Upsilon(r)}.

By

⁢

T

(
J
)

(
Υ
)

, we have

\displaystyle\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{(J)}_{X}(\phi)=\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\int_{X}\lvert\phi(x)-\phi(y)\rvert^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\int_{X\backslash B(x_{0},A_{2}r)}\int_{B(x_{0},A_{1}r)}\phi(y)^{p}K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

, which gives

hence

holds.

Lemma 11.10
.

Let
(\mathcal{E}^{(J)},\mathcal{F}^{(J)})
be a non-local regular
p
-energy. Let
\Omega\subseteq X
be a bounded open subset and let
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
satisfy
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}u\geq-1
in
\Omega
, that is,
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u;\varphi)\geq-\int_{X}\varphi\mathrm{d}m
for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)
. Then for any
M\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
u\wedge M\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
also satisfies that
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}(u\wedge M)\geq-1
in
\Omega
.

Proof.

Let
f\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
satisfy
0\leq f^{\prime}\leq 1
and
f^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
in
\mathbb{R}
. Then the following elementary result holds: for any
X,Y\in\mathbb{R}
and any
a,b\geq 0
, we have

\lvert f(X)-f(Y)\rvert^{p-2}\left(f(X)-f(Y)\right)(a-b)\geq\lvert X-Y\rvert^{p-2}(X-Y)\left(f^{\prime}(X)^{p-1}a-f^{\prime}(Y)^{p-1}b\right).

Indeed, without loss of generality, we may assume that
X>Y
, then there exists
Z\in[Y,X]
such that
f^{\prime}(Z)=\frac{f(X)-f(Y)}{X-Y}
. Since
0\leq f^{\prime}(X)\leq f^{\prime}(Z)\leq f^{\prime}(Y)
and
a,b\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\text{RHS}\leq(X-Y)^{p-1}\left(f^{\prime}(Z)^{p-1}a-f^{\prime}(Z)^{p-1}b\right)

\displaystyle=(X-Y)^{p-1}\left(\frac{f(X)-f(Y)}{X-Y}\right)^{p-1}(a-b)=\text{LHS}.

Since
0\leq f^{\prime}\leq 1
in
\mathbb{R}
, by the Markovian property, we have
f(u),f^{\prime}(u)^{p-1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
. For any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(f(u);\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert f(u(x))-f(u(y))\rvert^{p-2}\left(f(u(x))-f(u(y))\right)(\varphi(x)-\varphi(y))K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{X}\int_{X}\lvert u(x)-u(y)\rvert^{p-2}(u(x)-u(y))\left(f^{\prime}(u(x))^{p-1}\varphi(x)-f^{\prime}(u(y))^{p-1}\varphi(y)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt\cdot K^{(J)}(x,y)m(\mathrm{d}x)m(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u;f^{\prime}(u)^{p-1}\varphi).

By the Markovian property again, we have
f^{\prime}(u)^{p-1}\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)
is non-negative. By assumption, we have

\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u;f^{\prime}(u)^{p-1}\varphi)\geq-\int_{X}f^{\prime}(u)^{p-1}\varphi\mathrm{d}m\geq-\int_{X}\varphi\mathrm{d}m,

where the second inequality follows from that facts that
\varphi
is non-negative and that
0\leq f^{\prime}\leq 1
in
\mathbb{R}
. Hence
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(f(u);\varphi)\geq-\int_{X}\varphi\mathrm{d}m
for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}\cap C_{c}(\Omega)
. By Lemma
3.5
, this inequality holds for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)
.

For any
M\in\mathbb{R}
, by the Markovian property, we have
u\wedge M\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(J)}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
. Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
,
x\mapsto x\wedge M
, then there exists
\{f_{n}\}_{n}\subseteq C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
with
0\leq f^{\prime}_{n}\leq 1
and
f^{\prime\prime}_{n}\leq 0
in
\mathbb{R}
for any
n
, such that
f_{n}\uparrow g
uniformly in
\mathbb{R}
. By above, for any non-negative
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{(J)}(\Omega)
, we have
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(f_{n}(u);\varphi)\geq-\int_{X}\varphi\mathrm{d}m
. Letting
n\to+\infty
, by the dominated convergence theorem, we have
\mathcal{E}^{(J)}(u\wedge M;\varphi)\geq-\int_{X}\varphi\mathrm{d}m
, that is,
-\Delta_{p}^{(J)}(u\wedge M)\geq-1
in
\Omega
.
∎
